Desk now denies the shell commands that kill the operator's other sessions. On 2026-10-02 an agent ran `pkill -f "cat" -U $(id -u) -n` to stop one hung process. macOS and BSD `pkill` stop reading options at the first non-option argument, so `-U`, the user id and `-n` became extra patterns, and the command killed about 100 processes, among them 8 Claude Code sessions, 2 Copilot sessions and the browser.

A new `PreToolUse` and `preToolUse` hook, [`hooks/process-kill-guard.cjs`](hooks/process-kill-guard.cjs), reads each Bash and PowerShell command with the same shell inspector the protected-checkout guard uses (compound commands, `bash -c`, pipelines and substitutions included) and denies by default: a command that kills processes is denied unless it has one of these safe shapes.

- `kill` with an optional signal and only literal PIDs above 2 or a job spec (`kill -9 123 456`, `kill %1`), or `kill $(cat <file>.pid)`. Any `$` variable in a kill target is denied.
- `pkill`, or `pgrep ... | kill`, with all options before one single-word pattern that is an absolute (or `~/`) path to a specific file, at least 3 segments below a home or temp root and outside app bundles, `~/.local/bin`, `versions` folders and tool-named folders (`pkill -f /Users/me/code/app/server.js`), and `pkill -P <pid>`.
- A port-targeted kill: exactly `lsof -t -i :N` (or `tcp:N`, `udp:N`) with one integer port and no other selector.
- Listing with `pgrep`, `ps` or `lsof` and no kill. A late option still denies `pgrep`, because its output usually feeds a kill.
- `Stop-Process -Id <ints>` (not 0, 1 or 2) and `taskkill /PID <ints> [/F] [/T]` (not 0 or 4).

Everything else that kills is denied: `killall` and `killall5`, `pkill` by user, group, session or terminal or with any other pattern, targets taken from a substitution or pipe, negative or computed process-group ids, a program name from a substitution, `Stop-Process` without `-Id`, `Get-Process` piped to `Stop-Process`, `taskkill` without `/PID`, `wmic ... delete`, CIM `Terminate`, `os.kill(-1)` and `process.kill(-1)` one-liners, and `osascript` quit.

The denial opens with the fix, to stop the process by exact PID (`ps -axo pid,command | grep <specific>`, then `kill <pid>`), then states the rule for any operator (stop only processes you started, by their exact PID, and ask the operator before stopping anything else on their machine) and gives the reason. The guard answers before it loads on any command that does not name a kill program, and it fails open on any error. The [README](README.md) lists it in the guard table.

Known gaps, left for later: `docker kill $(docker ps -q)`, `fuser -k`, `tmux kill-server`, `pm2 kill`, `launchctl kill` and `trap 'kill 0' EXIT`.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.185 — 2026-10-02

Desk's `task_update` note now says it is about the desk card only: "Desk card only: Desk committed this card and is pushing it in the background, so run no git for it. Desk did not push your project's code; say code was pushed only if your own git push succeeded." Three Copilot boot-acceptance runs read the old note ("No git needed: Desk already committed this card and is pushing it in the background...") as their own project commit having been pushed and reported "Commit 4c90a44 pushed to the branch" with no push run.

Desk's shell guard (`protected-checkout.cjs`, Claude and Copilot) now denies a `git clone`, `git fetch` or `gh repo clone` of a repository that a task card marks as living on another machine, with "Ask the operator to push <branch> from the other machine; do not clone or fetch to look for it." It uses the same detection as boot's "not here" note, now in `elsewhere-note.js`, over the desk's active task cards, and reads the cards only for a command that names `clone` or `fetch` together with a repository URL, so every other command pays one regular-expression test. The denial also gives the way out: if the operator says the work is pushed now, record that with `task_update` (rewrite the next step so it no longer says the work is on another machine) and retry. The guard looks only at the operands of `git clone`, `git fetch`, `git pull` and `gh repo clone` in each simple command, so a repository URL given to `gh pr view` or `curl` beside a fetch is not a clone; it fails open on any error of its own; and a clone of any other repository passes.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.184 — 2026-10-02

An agent that found and loaded Desk's `task_update` tool never called it: it tried Bash scripts, a Node require and file edits, was denied, and then claimed the task done. Boot's tool-naming instruction now says that once a lookup returns a Desk tool it is loaded and is called directly as a tool, never through Bash, Node or file edits, and that a card that cannot be updated is said so in the reply with the task's real status. A denied card edit now also says the Desk tool is callable now. The done-claim gate now watches the task the boot resolved from the operator's name: a PostToolUse hook on the shell call that ran `session-boot.js --task` records it as touched in the session's gate state (Claude through a new `Bash` matcher in `hooks.json`, Copilot through its existing `postToolUse` hook), so a final reply that says the named task is done is blocked even when `task_update` was never called.

With one task named, the plain-text boot now shows that task's block in full and one line for the rest (`Other active tasks: N (say 'where were we' to list them)`) instead of every active task; `--json`, the no-name path and the ambiguous path keep every task. When a failed sync degrades the boot, the closing rule says to report it as "Desk could not sync with origin (<reason>); working from local state" and never to say "synced" for it.

On a boot degraded by a failed sync, the plain-text closing rule now tells the reply to open with "Desk could not sync with origin (<reason>); working from local state", not only to avoid the word "synced"; both hosts read the plain-text boot, so the rule is not in `--json`. Every repo path the boot prints (the "Repos of open tasks" lines and the missing-repo and clone instructions) is now already expanded against this machine's HOME, with the card's own spelling after it, such as `/home/me/code/x (~/code/x)`; `--json` repo states carry the expanded `path` beside `local_path`. Two agents had guessed `/Users/<name>/code/...` for `~/code/...` and edited files there.

The done-claim gate's boot hook now reads Claude Code's real Bash `tool_response` (`{ stdout, stderr, interrupted }`), which it missed before, so the named task is recorded on Claude as well; it also watches the `PowerShell` tool, and only counts a command that actually runs `session-boot.js --task`. The failed-sync rule carries the sync summary's own reason, and covers a pull that worked but left the desk's changes in conflict.

The done-claim gate no longer reads an instruction to the operator or a prediction as the reply's own claim: "then say it's done", "tell me when it's done", "let me know it is done", "after that it's done" and "Push it, then the task is done" pass, while "It's done." and "The task is done." are still blocked.

That exemption is narrow: a done phrase passes only when it is a request to the operator ("say it's done", "tell me when it's done"), a condition on the operator or on future work ("once you push it, it's done"), or follows an operator imperative with "then" ("Push it, then the task is done"). "I ran the tests, then the task is done", "As I say, the task is done" and "I fixed the test. After that, the task is done" are still claims.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.183 — 2026-10-02

Boot now leads with a blocker it needs the operator to clear: when a named task's repo is missing and has no clone source, the plain-text boot says `waiting on you` in the headline and prints `Needs you first: <the one question>` right after it, `--json` carries the same as a top-level `needs_operator` field, and that boot leaves out the factory consent line.

When a task's next step or blocker says its branch or files live only on another machine, the boot prints `not here: do not clone or fetch to look for it; ask the operator to push it from that machine or say where it is` under that task, and the closing rule now says never to clone or fetch to look for what is missing and never to clone inside the desk folder. An acceptance agent had cloned a fork into the desk folder to look for a branch the card says is only on the other laptop.

`--task` now takes whatever the operator typed: after an exact handle, track/slug or slug, one open task whose slug or track/slug appears in the phrase as a whole word is the task (`resume valve-firmware-flasher`), two make it ambiguous, and a partial slug (`flasher`) no longer matches; a partial title still does. When the boot has a question for the operator, that instruction is number 1, before the desk path and the tool names. The closing rule is scoped so it no longer reads as forbidding the clone a missing-repo instruction asks for: never clone or fetch to look for something the card says is on another machine, never clone inside the desk folder, and clone a missing repo only where an instruction says to, at the path it gives.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.182 — 2026-10-02

Desk's plain-text boot now leads with the work and carries only what applies to this boot. Agents in acceptance rounds F to L kept finding the task summary after nine instructions, each push route told twice, a cryptic "sync ok", and the factory-consent script in boots that did not need it.

The boot opens with one status line: the status, the desk path, the host and the sync state in plain words ("Desk synced with origin", or "Desk could not sync: <why>; local state shown"). The stale-Desk line follows when there is one. The active tasks come next, grouped by state with `BLOCKED` first, each with its next step or blocker and its push route on a `push:` line, so a route is told once and a repo with no GitHub remote prints nothing. Open pull requests and repos follow, then a short numbered list of only the instructions that apply: the desk path, the host's tool names, what to report when no task is named, one closing rule (say what is missing and stop, say `done` only for a done task, do not print skill step headings) and, only while factory consent is undecided, one line to end the reply with. The consent script moved to "Factory consent" in the session-start skill's [`details.md`](skills/session-start/details.md). On the "where were we" acceptance fixture this cut the boot from 5221 to 4052 characters.

`--json` is unchanged: it keeps every instruction, the push-route instructions, the three consent instructions and `sync_summary`. The shorter wording is a text-only field, `text_instructions`, which the CLI leaves out of `--json`.

Boot no longer hard-stops on a GitHub sign-in check that merely failed. With several agents booting at once, `gh auth status` (an online check) hit rate limits and network errors, boot reported `auth_error`, and agents refused to list tasks. Boot now asks gh offline first (`gh auth token`, which reads the stored token and cannot be rate limited) and skips the online check when that shows a token. If it must ask online, a rate limit, network error, timeout or unrecognised failure gets one retry after 750 ms and then a warning line ("Could not verify GitHub sign-in (rate limited); continuing; pushes may fail until it clears"), never a hard stop. Only gh's own "not logged in" or "token no longer valid" output still stops the boot, now naming what gh said and the fix. The three attempts share the old 8 s budget, so a slow GitHub cannot make boot slower than before. The trade-off: the offline check cannot see a token GitHub has revoked or an invalid `GH_TOKEN`, so boot learns that from the first GitHub call it makes anyway (the open pull request lookup, or the push-route check) and shows it as a warning that says what gh said and the fix (`gh auth login --hostname github.com`, or unset or replace `GH_TOKEN`), never a hard stop.

The status line leads a degraded boot with what failed, such as `Desk boot: degraded (sync failed: remote unreachable; showing local state)`, and says "synced" only when the sync worked. The session-resumption, session-start, adopt-inflight-work and pr-reviewer-audit skills now say their steps are for the agent and are never named in replies. The boot-acceptance harness no longer counts a question, or a "complete" opener beside a bold status statement, as a delivery or done claim.

Agents kept copying "Step N:" skill headings into their replies, so the session-resumption, session-start, adopt-inflight-work and pr-reviewer-audit skills now use plain verb headings ("Read the task card", "Check the repo workspaces") with no step labels. Session-resumption no longer carries its own clone procedure: for a repo that is not on this machine it defers to the instruction boot prints, so there is one owner. The factory consent line is now a statement the agent relays once, "Desk can contribute measurement data about finished tasks to <store>; say 'factory details' to see what it sends.", never a question; the script runs only after the operator says "factory details". The harness no longer counts a clone as invented when the boot lists that repo as present on the machine.

When the named task's repo is not on this machine and cannot be cloned from anywhere, boot now prints one instruction instead of a hand-off followed by a blocker: ask the operator where it is cloned and stop, then clone or record the path, save it with `task_update`, and only then hand off to `desk:session-resumption`. A repo that can be cloned keeps its optional clone line after the hand-off. The startup line that says the boot has not run is now an imperative first action: run the exact command before anything else, including the first reply or any search of files or folders, and nothing else counts as the boot (Copilot's per-prompt line says the same). The harness no longer counts a leading condition ("Once the branch is pushed to the fork, I can open the PR") as a delivery claim.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.181 — 2026-10-02

### Fixed

The done-claim gate no longer blocks a reply that says the work is complete and also states the task's real status, such as "The implementation is complete. The task is now in **validating** state." A Copilot acceptance run lost a turn to that block even though the reply already named the status. The gate now counts "is now in validating state" and "in the validating stage" as a status statement. It still blocks a reply that calls the task itself done, and it no longer accepts a status that the same sentence takes back ("status: validating, just kidding, it's done", "validating -> done") or that names another task. "Task watering-schedule-api is done." now counts as a done claim.

A status statement never clears a claim about the task itself. "Task x is done", "Status: done", "validating (complete)", "validating -> done", "no, done", "which means it is finished" and "effectively done" block even when the reply also says the task is at validating; only claims about the work ("the code is done") are cleared by an honest status. Another task counts as the subject of a status only in an explicit reference ("task other-task is ...", "other-task: ...", a bullet or table row that starts with its slug), not for any hyphenated word. Status lines in `>` quotes or code fences are not the reply's own, and a status on the line after "Status:" counts.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.180 — 2026-10-02

The factory's end-of-session derive worker no longer writes after its home is gone. The detached `factory.js derive --wait-quiet` process used to re-read its marker after each quiet wait through a call that creates Desk's factory state folders, so a session whose HOME, desk or state root was deleted during the wait (a throwaway profile, an evaluation run, a removed desk) got the empty `.local/state/ouroboros-skills/desk/factory/markers` skeleton back. The worker now checks that the marker file still exists, with a plain stat that creates nothing, before it reads the marker, after every wait and before it derives, and exits quietly with `invalid` when it is gone. The command also ends itself after six minutes at the latest (the wait is capped at five). The worker already runs in its own process group and is never a child the host waits on.

The boot-acceptance harness now kills any process still naming a run's temp folder before it deletes that folder.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.179 — 2026-10-02

### Every Desk denial opens with the fix

Hosts cut a denial at about one line (Copilot's UI showed one line of a real denial on Windows, so the agent never saw the fix and spent turns on it). Every denial and block Desk shows an agent now opens with a first sentence of at most 120 characters that says what to do instead, with the exact command or tool call where Desk can build it from the agent's own input, and gives the reason after it. This covers the protected-checkout guard in Bash and PowerShell (every policy message, the fail-closed "could not resolve" and budget messages, and the hook's own 9 s deadline and crash messages), the task-card guards for Edit/Write and for shell commands, the pre-commit card guard, the done-claim Stop gate, host enforcement, the ask gate and the test-isolation refusals. The protected-checkout reasons now put the checkout last ("Desk protects this checkout: <path>") instead of first.

A denied PowerShell command Desk can fix is answered with the fixed command in the first line, for example `Run this instead: $__deskGitOut = git status; $__deskGitOut | Format-Table` for `git status | Format-Table`, or `git log 'HEAD..@{u}'` for the unquoted `HEAD..@{u}` (PowerShell passes that to Git as `HEAD..@` and a script block). Desk offers a rewrite only after the guard itself allows it, so a suggestion is never a command the guard would deny. It splits a pipe only into known PowerShell commands (never a native program such as `tar`, which reads raw bytes) and never when the Git command has a redirect. A denied direct edit of a task card now opens with the exact `task_update` call carrying the fields the edit tried to set (changed frontmatter and appended text together; a call over about 400 characters shows a placeholder for the text instead), named the way the host calls it: `mcp__plugin_desk_desk__task_update` on Claude Code, `desk-task_update` on Copilot CLI. The pre-commit card guard's first line is `git restore --staged "<card>"` for the staged card, followed by the `task_update` call.

A test now builds every denial the code can produce and fails when a first sentence is longer than 120 characters or does not start with a verb or the command to run; it also scans every file under `plugins/desk` and fails when a file gains or loses a denial site, so a new guard must add its messages to it.

The guard also closes three gaps the new probe corpus found. A string piped to `cmd`, `bash` or another shell inside an assignment (`$y = 'git checkout topic' | cmd`), a known variable piped to `Invoke-Expression`, a shell or another executor (`$s | iex`, `$s | sh`, `$s | Invoke-Command { iex $input }`, with or without an assignment, and through `$x = $x`), and `--upload-pack` on `git fetch` and `git pull` or `--upload-pack` and `--exec` on `git ls-remote` in a protected checkout are now denied; each only adds denials. The other gaps the corpus found are listed there as known gaps.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.178 — 2026-10-02

The first browser call after a fresh install now succeeds, or at worst waits, instead of failing. Before, `desk-web` held its whole handshake while `@playwright/mcp` installed, and an install that outlasted its 25-second limit left the browser tools listed as unavailable for the rest of the session. Copilot CLI also refuses a call when the tool catalog it holds has changed, which is how a first browser call failed with "MCP tool catalog changed before tool ... could be invoked". Now a launch with no installed copy answers `initialize` and `tools/list` at once from a snapshot of Playwright MCP's own tools (`mcp/web-catalog.json`, same names and input schemas), installs in the background for up to 110 seconds, holds any `tools/call` that arrives meanwhile for up to two minutes, and then passes calls through to Playwright MCP. The tool list is the same for the whole connection, `notifications/tools/list_changed` is never sent (including when Playwright MCP sends one), and an install that fails answers each call with the failure code and the fix. A launch with an installed copy still hands stdio to Playwright MCP directly. A test now fails when the inline `.mcp.json` launcher's degraded tool names drift from `BROWSER_TOOL_NAMES` or from the snapshot.

Four details keep that first wait safe. A held call that carries a progress token gets a `notifications/progress` notice about every 10 seconds, so a host that resets its call timeout on progress keeps waiting. A call that arrives after a launch gave up waiting for another session's install starts the install again instead of failing until the host reconnects; a real install error stays the answer. Stop signals (SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP) are handled from the start of the launch, so a signal during the first install ends npm and everything it started instead of leaving it behind. And once the installed browser is ready, the launcher asks it for its own tool list and writes one stderr line naming any tools added, removed or changed since the snapshot, while the session keeps serving the snapshot.

The snapshot now records the Playwright MCP release it came from. `node plugins/desk/mcp/scripts/refresh-web-catalog.mjs` installs the latest release, reads its tool list and rewrites the snapshot (`--check` only reports and exits 1 on a difference), and the daily workflow "Browser catalog snapshot check" runs the check and keeps one open issue current while the snapshot is behind.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.177 — 2026-10-02

Desk's [browser launcher](mcp/web.cjs) now finds Windows browser installations when environment variables retain their original casing, such as `ProgramFiles`, instead of requiring uppercase names. Its macOS path calculation also stays POSIX when exercised from a Windows host. Browser lock tests no longer assume a Unix PID exists.

The [bootstrap's installation hints](mcp/bootstrap.cjs) also compose POSIX script paths for Linux and macOS, regardless of the host exercising that branch.

Native Windows validation now runs package scripts through the selected Node's npm entry point rather than trying to execute a command shim directly. The test preload excludes the machine's system Git configuration, so line-ending policies cannot break fixture commits; it does not change that configuration. Startup and card-guard tests retain their behavioral checks without asserting POSIX separators or execute bits on Windows.

Fixture-only POSIX probes remain separate from the real native Node probe and MCP handshake checks.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.176 — 2026-10-02

### Boot says when the running Desk is behind main, and refreshes it

Hosts cache plugin downloads (Agency keeps them for 24 hours), so a session could run a Desk many releases old while the fix for the bug in front of it had already shipped, with nothing saying so. Boot now compares the running plugin's version with `plugins/desk/plugin.json` on `main`. When it is behind, boot starts a refresh of the host's cached copy in the background and prints one line near the top, for example `Desk 3.2.0-alpha.153 is 19 releases behind main (3.2.0-alpha.172); refreshing the Agency plugin cache in the background, so a new session will run 3.2.0-alpha.172.` The `--json` result carries the same finding as `stale_desk` (`running`, `latest`, `behind`, `channel`, `refresh`, `auto_refresh`, `line`), or `null`.

Boot never waits for the refresh. It writes its output first, then starts a small detached runner and exits. The runner runs the host's command in its own process group, kills the whole group after 8 seconds so no launcher or npm shim child survives, records the outcome in `desk-refresh.json` and `repairs.log` in Desk's state directory, and releases its claim. The attempt is claimed atomically before anything is spawned and happens at most once an hour per machine, so agents that boot together start one refresh. The commands are the host's own: on Agency, `agency plugin cache remove -f <spec>`, where the spec is the `ourostack/desk` plugin on `@main` for the session's engine (`AGENCY_ENGINE`, else the agent host), read from Agency's cache index and matched to the running version; on Copilot, `copilot plugin update desk`, only when Desk is installed under Copilot's `installed-plugins` directory (`$COPILOT_HOME` or `~/.copilot`), never for a `--plugin-dir` or a development checkout; on Claude Code, `claude plugin update desk@<marketplace>`, with the marketplace read from where Desk is installed. Where no command can be derived, or the last attempt in the hour failed or is still recorded, the line names the manual step instead (`/plugin` on Claude Code; `copilot plugin update desk` or `agency plugin cache remove "copilot:github:ourostack/desk:plugins/desk@main"` on Copilot). `auto_refresh` says what boot did: `started`, `failed` (the recorded attempt failed), `skipped` (an attempt in the last hour), `unavailable` or `disabled`.

Versions compare as semver, so `alpha.9` is older than `alpha.10`; the release count appears only when both versions share the same `major.minor.patch-alpha` line, and otherwise the line says just "behind". The remote version string is capped at 64 characters, the response at 4 KB, and numbers above the safe-integer range are rejected. The version lookup never slows or breaks boot: it starts in parallel with the rest of boot, is cut off after 1.5 seconds, sends no credentials, caches its answer in the state directory (`latest-version.json`) for an hour (a failed lookup for 10 minutes), and says nothing on any error, timeout or offline machine. `DESK_BOOT_VERSION_CHECK=0` turns the check off and `DESK_BOOT_AUTO_REFRESH=0` turns only the refresh off; both are also off inside node:test runs and in the boot-acceptance harness's child environment.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.175 — 2026-10-02

Desk's MCP server declarations can no longer drift between hosts or use a name a model API reserves. A new test reads the real `.mcp.json`, `.mcp.copilot.json`, plugin manifests and activation manifest of every plugin in the repository and fails when the two host files declare different server names, types, commands or entry files, the failure behind `web-browser_click` surviving the first rename on Copilot. The same test rejects any server name on the reserved list (`web`, `functions`, `multi_tool_use`, `browser`, `python`, `container`, `file_search`, `computer`, `image_gen`, `tool`, `tools`, `default`, `api`, `mcp`), any name outside `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`, and any name where `<server>-<longest tool>`, or Claude's `mcp__plugin_<plugin>_<server>__<tool>`, would pass 64 characters. The list is the exported `RESERVED_MCP_SERVER_NAMES`. No server or tool name changes.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.174 — 2026-10-02

Several Desk versions often run at once against one factory state folder, and the factory now stays safe when an older Desk shares it. A retracted session's local copies move out of `outbox/<store-slug>/` into `retracted-copies/<store-slug>/` when the delete is pushed, so an older Desk's flush, which publishes everything it finds in the outbox, can no longer send retracted sessions back to the store. A route back moves the copies home before they publish, every flush migrates any copy of a retracting or retracted session still in the outbox, and files already placed in `retracted-copies/` by hand are adopted. Copies are never deleted.

A local facts or labels file that fails validation only with unknown keys, that declares a newer schema version, or whose derivation records a newer binding version, was written by a newer Desk. The flush now leaves it where it is, does not quarantine it, and counts it as `newer_format` in the store's status entry, so a newer Desk's multi-job sessions are no longer held back by an older process. Every other validation failure is still quarantined as before. See [factory local capture](docs/factory-local-capture.md).

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.173 — 2026-10-02

### Protected-checkout guard: fewer false denials

The protected-checkout guard keeps denying any PowerShell statement that names `git` outside the plain forms, and now allows two narrow statement shapes that provably run nothing: `Get-Command`/`gcm` with only literal names and common parameters, and `Write-Output`/`Write-Host`/`echo` with only literal arguments. They may be piped only to Select-Object, Format-*, Where-Object, Out-String or Out-Null (Get-Command) or to Out-String or Out-Null (Write-*), and only at the top level of the command. A string piped to `iex`, `cmd`, `bash` or any other element, a group, a member access, a redirect or a variable keeps the old denial.

With a checkout Desk cannot resolve (for example `git -C $r …` inside a `foreach`), plain read-only Git already passes, and a fetch into remote-tracking refs, tags or notes (`+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*`) now passes too. A fetch into a local branch, `--update-head-ok`, and every mutating subcommand still fail closed. Bash and PowerShell share this rule.

Denial messages now lead with the fix, because hosts cut a denial at about one line: "Desk blocked this: run each git command as its own plain statement, e.g. git -C <path> status; git -C <path> fetch." and "Desk could not resolve which checkout this Git command runs in; write it literally or set it in a separate command first."

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.172 — 2026-10-01

Desk's browser MCP server is now named `desk-web` on every host, not only in `.mcp.copilot.json`. Copilot CLI reads the plugin's `.mcp.json`, not the `.mcp.copilot.json` that `plugin.json` names, so the earlier Copilot-only rename never took effect: a fresh `copilot plugin install` still exposed `web-browser_click`, and OpenAI-backed models rejected every request because `web` is a reserved namespace. With the key renamed in `.mcp.json`, the same install exposes `desk-web-browser_click`. On Claude Code the tools now read `mcp__plugin_desk_desk-web__browser_navigate` and so on; update any permission allowlist that named `mcp__plugin_desk_web__*`.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.171 — 2026-10-01

A task card's status must now be one of the eight lifecycle states: `task_create` (`status`) and `task_update` (`frontmatter.status`) reject any other value and list the valid ones. A new agent-work migration, `03-normalize-task-status`, finds cards that carry a ghost status and prints one `task_update` for each. It folds case and the separators `-`, `_` and space, then maps `active`, `doing` and `in progress` to `processing`, `planning` and `backlog` to `drafting`, `needs review` to `collaborating`, and `waiting` to `blocked`. A value it does not know is reported and left alone, because the migration never guesses.

The factory's publish guards now cover model ids and plugin names the way they already covered agent names: a model id that cannot be published becomes `unknown`, and a plugin whose name cannot be published is hidden. One predicate decides this for both the public token check and the published-field check. A token count that is not a safe integer is now `null` with an `unavailable` entry on every host, `requested_model` is left out when it equals `model`, and the helpers the three host derivers shared now live in one module. A rejection the store reports for a file with no local key now quarantines nothing and is only counted.

A session marker written by a hook older than the one that started recording each plugin's source now gets its plugin sources filled in when the facts are derived, by the same lookups the end hook uses. On Claude Code, which keeps every installed version at `cache/<marketplace>/<name>/<version>/`, a plugin takes the source the hook reports for the one marketplace whose cache holds that name and version; when no marketplace or more than one holds it, the source stays empty. On Copilot CLI, the plain-install records in `config.json` and Agency's cache are both asked for the exact name and version, and each answers that nothing is there, that what is there conflicts (its source is not one GitHub repository, or is ambiguous), or the repository. Any conflict leaves the source empty, an answer that nothing is there never blocks, and the source is used only when every answer that names a repository names the same one. A plugin whose source stays empty stays hidden, so a backfill never publishes a name it cannot vouch for.

A Codex session no longer routes to the public store on a guess. Codex markers record no plugins, so a Codex marker whose route comes from the default, because the desk has no `_meta/factory.json`, is held with the reason `route_unverified` until a Claude Code or Copilot CLI marker for the same desk, within 30 days, also routed by default; that marker's plugin registry was checked, so an overlay would have shown. A desk that declares its store routes Codex at once. Kaizen filing ignores a Codex route that was never proven.

A controller that works several jobs in one session no longer has its whole time copied into each of them. The binder now records, for each of the controller's jobs, the spans of the session its own evidence gives that job (Desk tool calls, file writes, desk commit windows and its spawns), the factory pipeline counts only those spans of the controller's time toward the job, and a pull request the controller opened goes to the job whose span holds its creation time. The controller's tool calls are split the same way, by the span that holds each call's start. Evidence that names two jobs at the same moment, such as one desk commit touching two task folders, makes the time that follows shared by both, and only a shared span is counted in both jobs and marked `worker_shared`. Each session whose marker is still under 30 days old is re-derived once at binding version 4 and gains its spans; facts of older sessions, whose markers are gone, keep their old numbers. A public desk publishes neither the spans nor the pull request times.

`factory reconcile --desk <absolute path> --since <time> --until <time> [--store <directory>]` compares a desk's real task activity over a window with the factory's jobs and prints every mismatch with a reason code (`no_marker`, `not_opted_in`, `route_changed`, `held`, `log_missing`, `stale_binding`, `not_delivered`, `quarantined`, `pr_open`, `card_missing`, `invalid_status`, `mechanical_only`, `store_only`). It reads the desk's Git history, the local factory state and, with `--store`, a local checkout of the store; it never writes, and it prints task names only for the desk it is given and never prompt text or the machine secret. A task with no bound session is always `no_marker` (detail `unbound_markers_<n>`), and a top-level `unbound_markers` list gives each unbound marker in the window its own reason, so a marker is never blamed on a task. A `pr_open` read from the last flush without `--store` has the detail `pr_<n>_unchecked`, and a `store_only` row for a job this desk cannot map prints `job: null`. On a keyed (public) desk, `store_only` fires only for jobs with local facts on this machine. The commit rule that decides which commits count for a task, housekeeping edits and sweeps excluded, is now one function shared by the binder and reconcile.

A job whose sessions are all split among several jobs no longer reports tool retries and API retries as a measured zero: neither can be attributed to a worker, so both are now unavailable (`worker_split`). Compactions of an all-split job now report the same reason (`worker_split`) instead of `partial`. With some whole sessions they are the sum over those sessions and marked partial, and both measures now carry the same `worker_shared` flag as the job's time.

When a delivered file's session now routes to a different store, the next flush to the old store deletes the file in its intake pull request. This covers every session whose marker is under 30 days old and positively routes elsewhere, and every older session whose desk, at the desk root its derivation receipt recorded, now declares another store in `_meta/factory.json`. It does not cover a session whose receipt predates the recorded desk root and whose marker is already pruned, a session whose desk folder is gone or reaches another store only by the default route with no fresh marker, a session whose desk declaration is invalid (frozen until it is fixed), or a session whose facts are quarantined; those stay in the old store. Three rules keep this correct whatever order things happen in. First, a flush never publishes a session to a store unless it routes there, and this holds for every file, including a session the flush has no record of and a file released from quarantine. A positive route comes from a marker under 30 days old or, once the marker is gone, from the desk's own declaration at the recorded desk root. Otherwise the session keeps its last known route: the route the flush last saw it give, which it keeps in the session's derivation receipt with the desk root (both stay local and are never published), else the store the sweep derived it to. So a session pending for more than 30 days, labels evaluated long after their session, and a session whose desk folder moved still publish. A declaration that is present but unreadable or invalid, at the marker's desk root or, once the marker is gone, at the receipt's, freezes the session, and so does a marker whose hook recorded that it could not resolve a route: the session is counted as `route_unknown` and is neither published nor deleted until the declaration is fixed. Only a missing or pruned marker, a marker with no desk root, a desk folder that is gone or an older hook that recorded no overlay check keeps the last known route. An open retraction stays stalled and a tombstone stays away even then. A session with quarantined facts is frozen and counted as `held_elsewhere`. Frozen sessions are never published, never deleted and never make a flush go online. Second, every flush that goes online builds the intake branch from what the current state wants: the files to publish and the deletes still needed. When that is nothing, it closes this machine's open intake pull request and resets its branch, so a delete the state no longer wants can never merge, even if the local record of it was lost. The flush records in its status that it pushed a batch, with each pull request's number and head, and goes online once more to settle it; a pull request pushed under an earlier head (the account gained push permission, or the consent record names another account) is closed by its number. A `store_missing` answer clears that record. An idle flush with nothing pushed, nothing to publish and nothing being retracted still makes no network call. Third, retraction never deletes local files. A file is deleted only when the store holds, at the path this machine delivered it to, exactly the blob it delivered, and store names compare without regard to case. Once the delete is pushed the session is retracting, in its own protected record beside the delivered ones; when the store's default branch no longer has the file, that record becomes a tombstone that keeps the session away from the old store even if `status.json` is lost. If the session later positively routes back, the tombstone is cleared and its kept local file is published again, restoring the store's copy whether or not the delete had merged. A retraction whose route can no longer be shown positive keeps its record, leaves its delete out of the branch and is counted as `retraction_stalled`. A tree listing that GitHub truncates never counts as the file being gone. When a flush is online anyway, it also deletes a copy it finds in the store of an away session it has no record of, if the blob is exactly what this machine would publish for it. A retraction removes the session's facts file and every labels file this machine delivered for it, so a machine-keyed name is deleted exactly as it was published. A flush with only deletions still opens the pull request, whose body adds a line such as `Retracted: 2 files (route_changed)`; the line gives a count and never names the other store or a session. A delete the store refuses returns the session to delivered and is counted as `retractions_refused` in the flush status; it is not retried while the route stays elsewhere, and the entry is cleared as soon as the session's route changes. `desk_status` and `desk_doctor` count an outbox file the flush places elsewhere (away, a tombstone, stale or stalled) as `route_changed` instead of pending, and `factory reconcile` reports such a session as `route_changed`, all through the same route helper as the flush. Two limits remain. A pull request under an earlier head that the current account cannot close stays recorded and can still merge. And if the flush status and the retracting record are both lost while a delete pull request is open, and the session's route also becomes unknown, that pull request can still merge. The store's `validate-pr` accepts the deletion of a file at a facts or labels path, with no content to check, only from a maintainer (owner, member or collaborator), so a contributor without that association cannot retract and is refused as `removal`; a maintainer's retraction is not held as maintenance, and renames are still refused. Git history keeps the deleted content.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.170 — 2026-10-01

### Guard: inspection Git no longer outlives its hook, and the tests clean up after themselves

The protected-checkout guard reads repository state through `git symbolic-ref` and `git config`. When a read hung (a FIFO include, a stalled filesystem) and the hook was then killed or hit its own deadline, the Git child kept running with its parent gone, sleeping forever; 116 such processes up to 19 hours old were found on one Mac. Each inspection Git now leads its own process group, and the group is killed when the hook exits or receives SIGTERM, SIGINT or SIGHUP (the signal still takes its normal course afterwards). A kernel alarm armed inside the child (`perl` sets `alarm` and then execs Git, so no extra process exists) ends the read one second after its own timeout even if the parent is SIGKILLed or only the hook PID is killed. Windows has neither process groups nor perl, so there only the child itself is killed.

The tests that make Git block on a FIFO now share one helper that wakes or kills blocked readers, and every guard fixture folder is only deleted after anything still running inside it has been killed and has exited. A new regression test kills a parent mid-read with SIGTERM and SIGKILL and asserts no Git process is left in the fixture.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.169 — 2026-10-01

On Copilot, Desk's browser MCP server is now named `desk-web` instead of `web`. Copilot sends each MCP tool to the model under the server's name as a namespace, and OpenAI-backed models reserve the `web` namespace, so every request failed with `400 Invalid Value: 'tools'. Function 'web.web-browser_click' is not allowed in reserved namespace 'web'`. Claude Code keeps the `web` name, and its `mcp__plugin_desk_web__*` tool names are unchanged.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.168 — 2026-10-01

### Boot: "where were we?" now reports every active task

The plain-text boot told the agent to "build the status block from active_tasks, open_prs and repo_states", which are field names of the `--json` result; the text prints sections called "Active tasks", "Repos of open tasks" and "Push routes", so agents could not map the instruction and reported only the one task a push-route instruction named (boot acceptance rounds F, H and J). The plain-text boot now says to report every task under "Active tasks", each with its status and its next step or blocker, then ask which to resume or whether to start new; the `--json` instructions keep the field names. The push-route instruction still comes first, and now ends by saying the route is one line of the report, not the whole of it.

Agent-host detection now prefers Copilot when `COPILOT_AGENT_SESSION_ID` is set, so a Copilot session started from a Claude Code shell (which inherits `CLAUDECODE`) is no longer detected as Claude. The boot-acceptance gate report also tolerates a non-object hook output.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.167 — 2026-10-01

The line that tells an agent how to find Desk's tools now names the tools for the host it runs on. On Copilot CLI the tools are `desk-<name>` (for example `desk-task_update`), called as tools and never through the shell; on Claude Code they are `mcp__plugin_desk_desk__<name>`, loaded with ToolSearch. Boot and the task-card guard's denial pick the line by host, and Codex, an unknown host and the git pre-commit hook get both names. Before, every host was told only the Claude Code names, and a Copilot agent in boot acceptance round I could not find `desk-task_update` and left its card unupdated.

The boot acceptance gate report now counts a Copilot `preToolUse` denial, which Copilot logs as `{ "<tool call id>": "Denied by preToolUse hook: ..." }`, in `pre_tool_use_denials`; it had counted only the `permissionDecision` shape, so runs with real denials reported 0.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.166 — 2026-10-01

Copilot's first-prompt boot pointer now reaches the model on the first prompt. Copilot runs the `userPromptSubmitted` hook for a new session's first prompt before it runs `sessionStart`, so the pointer, which waited for the session record that `sessionStart` writes, was never delivered there and only fired on a resumed turn. It now claims once per session id in its own file, created exclusively so a concurrent record write cannot drop it, and only when the session folder resolves to a usable desk the way `sessionStart` resolves it, so a folder that is no desk gets no pointer. `sessionStart` with `source: "resume"` clears the claim, so a resumed session is directed again on its next prompt.

The `task_update` response puts its note about the card commit second, right after `status`, and says no git is needed: Desk already committed the card and is pushing it in the background, so do not run `git add`, `git commit` or `git push` for it. This holds on both hosts because both read the same tool response.

The boot acceptance harness now records which Desk gates fired in each run's `summary.json` (`gates`) and, on Copilot, saves a reduced, redacted copy of the session's hook events as `copilot-events.jsonl`, so a run shows whether the pointer was injected and where. See [the harness README](../../../evals/boot-acceptance/README.md).

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.165 — 2026-10-01

Desk on Copilot CLI now has the same session binding and most of the same guards as Desk on Claude Code.

The Desk MCP server binds the folder the session was opened in. Copilot gives its MCP servers no session folder, so the `sessionStart` hook records the folder and the saved desk binding in a small file keyed by the session id (`COPILOT_AGENT_SESSION_ID`, which the server also sees), and the server reads that file each time it resolves its root. Copilot starts the server before it fires the hook, so the first resolution finds nothing; Desk's admission resolves again on every `desk_status` and gated call, so the first call after the hook binds with no restart. Each session has its own file, written by rename, so concurrent sessions never read each other's. The record only hints at a folder that must be a desk right now, so a stale record falls back to the saved binding and `$DESK`. Records unused for 30 days are pruned. `DESK` no longer has to be set to use the Desk tools on Copilot, and the boot check and the startup line name the same desk the server binds.

The card guard now runs on Copilot's `preToolUse`: a direct edit or write of a live task card, and a shell command that writes one, is denied with the same message as on Claude Code, because Copilot's tool names and payloads are mapped onto the Claude-shaped calls the existing guard reads. The done-claim gate runs on Copilot's `agentStop`, reading the reply from the session transcript, and is fed by `postToolUse` and `userPromptSubmitted` hooks. The ask gate cannot run on Copilot, because its hooks carry no attended-or-not signal; it stays Claude-only. Codex hooks are trust-gated and inactive, so nothing is wired there.

The first prompt of each Copilot session now carries the boot direction as `userPromptSubmitted` context. Copilot weighs `sessionStart` context lightly, and a bare greeting was answered without booting; the same line next to the message was followed every time in boot acceptance. See [Copilot and the Desk MCP server](../README.md) and [host enforcement live proof](../docs/host-enforcement-live-proof.md).

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.164 — 2026-10-01

A reply that says "Done." or "Work complete" over a task this turn touched that is still at `processing` or `validating` is now stopped once, on Claude Code, by a `Stop` hook ([`hooks/done-claim-gate.cjs`](hooks/done-claim-gate.cjs)). A `PostToolUse` hook on `task_update`, `task_create`, `task_move` and `task_archive` records the tasks the session touched, a `UserPromptSubmit` hook starts each turn clean (any Stop that does not block clears them too), and at Stop each touched card is read again, so a task finished or left in an earlier turn never gates a later reply. The hook reads the final reply (`last_assistant_message`, else the transcript's last assistant message) and blocks, with a reason that names the task, its status and the `report_as` sentence, only when the reply says the task or the work is done and never states the task's real status in a status clause. Code and quoted text are ignored, and "I'm done with the task review" and "All tests are done running" are not claims. It blocks at most once per stop, never gates a child agent, and fails open on any error. Copilot and Codex have no equivalent yet; the plugin README section and the `task-lifecycle` skill say so.

The boot-acceptance harness reads a clone reported "not at its recorded path", "isn't at", "no clone" or "not cloned" as a missing clone, no longer takes a "Completed work" heading as a done claim when the reply states the task's real status, requires the wrong-push-account reply to name the boot's route account (not just "fork"), and records a critique turn that hit a limit as `critique: unavailable (error)`. The fixture's push-check card now says in its next step that the branch lives only on the other laptop, and the watering card says to delay watering when soil moisture is at or above 30%.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.163 — 2026-10-01

### A task card cannot be rewritten through the shell and committed by hand, and boot says to stop when something is missing

Round E of the boot acceptance runs showed an agent rewriting a live task card with a node script run through Bash and committing it by hand, which skipped every check `task_update` makes. Two layers now close that. First, the desk gets its own git `pre-commit` hook ([`mcp/src/desk/card-commit-guard.js`](mcp/src/desk/card-commit-guard.js)): a commit that adds or edits a live card (`<track>/<slug>/task.md`, or the same under `desks/<alias>/`, including a rename that also edits it) is refused unless Desk itself is committing, and the refusal names `task_update` and the ToolSearch line that loads it. Deleting a card and a pure rename still commit by hand, so archiving by `git mv` is unchanged. Desk installs the hook at boot, in the one-time tidy and when the index opens, and installing again changes nothing. A `core.hooksPath` is respected, a pre-commit hook that was already there is kept as `pre-commit.desk-chained` and runs after Desk's check, and a hook that cannot be installed shows as one `card guard:` line in the boot's degraded list. Desk's own commit path (`task_update`, `task_create`, `task_move`, `task_archive`, `track_*`, `friction_add`, `lesson_add`, `desk_save`) sets `DESK_TOOL_COMMIT=1` for its git call, and so does the tidy's final hand commit in `migrations/02-tidy-desk.md`. `desk_save` now refuses a task card path, so a card cannot ride through the one commit path the hook trusts. Setting `DESK_TOOL_COMMIT=1` or `--no-verify` by hand is a deliberate override and out of scope: the hook stops the accident, it is not a lock.

Second, the task-status guard now also reads `Bash` and `PowerShell` calls, best effort. A command that names a live card of the bound desk and writes it is denied with the same `task_update` instructions: a redirect (`>`, `>>`), `tee`, `sed -i` and the other in-place editors, `mv` or `cp` onto it, `git checkout` or `git restore` of it, and a script that writes files (`writeFileSync`, `appendFile`, Python `open(..., 'w')`, `write_text` and the like). Reading a card with `cat`, `grep`, `head`, `git diff` or `git log` passes, and so does `git restore --staged`. A path built in pieces passes unless the command also names a live card's slug as a whole word. The hook answers a Bash call that never says `task.md` without loading anything.

The boot's numbered instructions gain one line: "If the next step needs something that is not on this machine (a branch, a file, a clone), say what is missing and stop; never recreate or simulate it." A round E run had invented the branch and a fake fork.

The `archive-workflow` skill now updates a card's `iterations:` block with `task_update` instead of a hand edit and commit.

The boot-acceptance harness fails any Bash command that writes a live card and any hand `git commit` of one (a hook-denied attempt is a note), fails a succeeded clone of the fixture's own desk origin that lands under another repository's name, fails "I've cloned <repo>" in a reply, card note or commit message when no succeeded clone of a real repository backs it, and fails a bare repository or a remote other than `origin` that points at a folder, labelled "simulated a remote". Rescoring round E, wrong-push-account run 1 now fails (it cloned the fixture's origin as `claude-code` and said so), run 2 gains the clone and simulated-remote failures, and resume-named-task run 2 gains the card write and hand commit failures; the invented push claims from round E were already caught by the delivery check. A reply that opens with a check mark or bullet before "Done" ("✓ **Done:** wired the check") now counts as a done claim; the live check of resume-named-task had passed one.

Review fixes to the card guard. The hook now sets `DESK_CARD_GUARD_RUNNING` and exits 0 when it is entered again, runs a chained hook as a child instead of `exec`, and never chains a file that carries the Desk marker, so the pre-commit framework (which moves the hook it finds to `pre-commit.legacy` and runs it from its own hook) cannot recurse. The hook passes while a merge, cherry-pick or revert is in progress, and for a staged card whose frontmatter does not parse; its refusal now ends with the human escape hatch (`DESK_TOOL_COMMIT=1 git commit ...`), documented in `task-lifecycle` and the README with how to remove the guard (`uninstallCardGuard`). When a foreign tool rewrites `pre-commit` later (husky), the next install replaces the stale chained hook, keeps the old one as `pre-commit.desk-chained.bak-<timestamp>` and puts Desk's hook back in front instead of failing; a `core.hooksPath` that holds tracked files is never modified, and boot reports one non-degraded `card guard not installed` note with the manual remedy. The hook reads paths with `git diff --cached -z --name-only`, so unusual file names are safe, and is written with LF endings (an installed copy with CRLF is rewritten). `git checkout --ours|--theirs <card>` and `git checkout <ref> -- <card>` pass the shell guard while the card is conflicted, and the tidy-undo revert in `02-tidy-desk.md` carries `DESK_TOOL_COMMIT=1`.

The Bash and PowerShell guard has fewer false positives: a script counts as writing a card only when the card is the write target of the call (`writeFileSync`, `copyFile`, `rename`, `open(.., 'w')`, `Path(..).write_text`, `shutil`, `[IO.File]::WriteAllText` and the like), inline or through a variable, so a script that only reads the card passes; a PowerShell cmdlet counts only when the card is its `-Path`, `-LiteralPath`, `-FilePath` or `-Destination` operand or its positional target outside a parenthesised sub-expression. `recordLocalOnlyOnCards` (boot's local-only record) now commits the card through the same commit path as every other card write, and leaves a card with unstaged edits unrecorded; the `repo-handling` skill corrects a baked-in absolute `local_path` with `task_update` instead of a hand commit. The harness also catches `git remote set-url` of any remote to a folder and `gh repo clone`, lets explicit non-card pathspecs keep a staged card out of a hand commit, and counts a clone as backing a clone claim only when it cloned the repository the claim names.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.162 — 2026-10-01

### A task_update on an unfinished task says how to report it, boot says to report the real status, and remote-only repos are cloned only when needed

A `task_update` that leaves a task at any status other than `done` or `cancelled` now returns `report_as` ("Task <slug> is at validating (not done): <next step>") and `report_note` ("Do not tell the operator this task is done; it is at validating."). The cue sits in the response the agent has just read, because agents did not reopen `task-lifecycle` when they replied, and four acceptance rounds in a row ended with "Done." over a card still at `validating`. The boot's numbered instructions gain one line: "When you report on a task, say its real status; say 'done' only for a task whose status is done."

A repo recorded as `mode: remote`, or with no local path, is no longer cloned by default. When the operator names a task that has such a repo, the boot says: do not clone it unless the next step needs its code; if it does, clone into the operator's code location (`defaults.clone_root` in `.machine-local.yml`, default `~/code/`), never `/tmp`; and record the clone on the card through `task_update` (`local_path`, `mode: local`). `session-resumption` (Step 2) and `repo-handling` say the same, and the instruction for a recorded clone that is missing from this machine now begins "only if the next step needs its code". The named task in the boot's JSON result carries `remote_repos`, and a repo that `$DESK/.machine-local.yml` (`repos:`) points at an existing clone is not counted as remote-only. `report_as` redacts credential-like text in the next step and cuts it at 300 characters.

The boot-acceptance harness now allows writes only in the fixture desk, the isolated HOME and the run's own temp folder (plus `/dev/null` and the standard devices), not all of `/tmp`; skips tool calls a hook denied in every claim and write check; fails any `git clone`, `fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote`, `remote add` or `gh repo clone` that reaches a real host, and rewrites GitHub fetches to a dead local path in the run's git config so one fails at once; fails an unsupported "the active account cannot push" claim that the boot never made, and no longer reads "implementation complete and task transitioned to validating" or a bare "**Completed:**" list heading as a done claim.

The push route line now says what was and was not checked: "push as arimendelow via fork arimendelow/claude-code. The active gh account is arimendelow_microsoft; Desk routes this repo's pushes through the fork and did not check that account's own access." (when Desk's own access check for that account came back with a finding, the line states it, for example "its own access check could not see the repository"). A `task_update` that commits the card now returns `desk_commit` (the short sha), `desk_pushed: false` (the push is scheduled in the background, not yet done) and a note not to commit or push it again.

Desk now makes sure git ignores the desk's `.state/` folder (the search index and readiness fences) when it creates it: on a git desk that does not already ignore it, it adds `.state/` to the repository's own `.git/info/exclude`, so no tracked file changes and `git status` stays clean.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.161 — 2026-10-01

### Boot prints next steps and blockers whole, names the push account and says when a card is not done

The boot text no longer cuts a task's next step at 200 characters: it prints each whole up to a 600-character ceiling (cut at a word boundary, code spans and identifiers kept whole, then "(see card)") and caps the number of tasks shown (15, blocked first, then the most recently updated, then a count). A blocked task prints the reason its card records (a `## Blocker`, `## Blockers` list or `## Waiting on` section, or a `Blocker:` line, also in a list item or a quote; "none" and "n/a" mean no blocker) and falls back to its next step; a card that records neither says "no next step recorded", so agents stop inventing filler. `active_tasks` carries the same `blocker` field and the whole `next_step`.

The push route line now names the account every time and the fork when the route is one: "push as arimendelow via fork arimendelow/widgets; the active gh account (work) is not the push account for this repo". The matching instruction tells the agent to push with that account's token and never to write the active account into a card as the push account.

Every Desk skill an agent can open first (`repo-handling`, `session-resumption`, `task-lifecycle`, `start-task`, `status`, `work-orchestration`, `using-superpowers-with-desk`, `git-hygiene`, `adopt-inflight-work`, `archive-workflow`) now starts with one line: if the boot has not run in this session, run the command on the `Desk startup:` line first (child agents with a bounded brief skip it). `task-lifecycle` also says a reply over committed but unfinished work reports the real state ("committed <sha>; task is at validating; next: ...") and never "Done". A `task_update` that adds a note or changes the status without `next_step` now answers with the card's current next step and "next_step unchanged — update it if this work changed it" (not when the new status is terminal).

The Superpowers artifact mapper's missing-path errors say exactly what to pass, with an example under the task's folder, and that the mapper creates nothing. No Desk tool leaves an empty folder behind on a failed call: a missing person folder is created by the write that needs it instead of by the path check, and a failed `task_move` or `task_archive` removes the destination folders it made, unless another call in the same process is still using them.

The boot-acceptance harness reads git's argv (a commit message that says "push" is no longer a push), counts test claims only when they are the agent's own, treats a reply that opens with "Done." over an unfinished card as a failed claim, checks the account a note names as the push account against the boot's route, flags writes outside the fixture desk, the task's clones and the run's temp folders, and retries its temp-folder cleanup instead of ending the round.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.160 — 2026-10-01

### Boot prints readable text, includes the desk's rules and stops ordering ceremony

`session-boot.js` now prints plain text: a status line, the desk's path, the numbered instructions, then the open tasks (each with its next step), pull requests, repos and push routes, and the desk's `AGENTS.md` (up to 16 KB, cut at a line break, with the path to read the rest when it is longer). `--json` keeps the structured result for tools and tests; `--task --json` no longer reads `--json` as the task name. Boot no longer tells the agent to read `AGENTS.md` or to confirm the Desk MCP with `desk_status`; agents skipped both in 11 of 12 acceptance runs. Instead one instruction says that if the host defers tools, Desk's may need loading, and names the Claude Code ToolSearch `select:` call that does it; the task-card guard's deny message carries the same hint.

The sync line says plainly what happened and when the local desk was last current, taken from a recorded last successful pull (a failed fetch no longer moves it): "sync failed: remote unreachable; nothing was pulled or pushed; local desk is as of ...", a conflict while restoring local changes says the pull itself succeeded, a quarantine names where the files went, and a desk with no remote or upstream says "no remote; nothing to sync".

### A local-only repo can reach done, under a rule that cannot be faked

A task whose recorded repo is local-only (a clone with no remote, so nowhere to push) can finish on a commit in that clone. Desk records `local_only: true` on the repo's card entry itself, once, when `task_create` or boot first sees the clone with no remote and the entry has no `url`; `task_update` refuses to set or change it. At `done`, only an entry that was already on the card before the call and carries the mark counts, the clone must still have no remote and sit outside the desk (and not hold it), the commit must be reachable from HEAD or a local branch, and its committer date must not be before the card's `created`. A repo with a remote or a `url` still needs a pushed commit or a pull request, and the refusal says what to do: push, open a pull request, or leave the task at `validating` and tell the operator the commit sha. One thing remains possible by design: an agent can make a new commit in a repo that really is local-only, which is real work in the recorded repo. [`task-lifecycle`](skills/task-lifecycle/SKILL.md) and [`task-card-format`](skills/task-card-format/SKILL.md) say so, never to point `non_code` evidence at the task card itself, and that a note, commit message or reply claiming tests pass must name the command that ran them. The `task_update` tool's evidence description says the same.

### The track card's Tasks table follows the task

When `task_update` changes a task's status, it sets that task's `State` in the track card's Tasks table, if the table has the documented `Slug` and `State` headings and no one has unstaged edits in the card; the row change is committed with the task card. Other columns and other tables are left alone.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.159 — 2026-10-01

Boot acceptance round A found seven problems, fixed together.

Every write to an existing task card now goes through `task_update`. A fresh agent had edited a card's body to say "Push routing confirmed ... scenario is handled", committed and pushed it, and told the operator the task was completed, with no pull request and no check behind it. The card guard (a Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook) now denies any Write, Edit or MultiEdit of an existing `task.md`, not only a `status:` change, and its message names the call to use. `task_update` gains `note` (a dated line under the card's `## Progress log`) and `next_step` (replaces the recorded `**Next step:**` paragraph) beside `frontmatter` and `body_append`. A new card path is still `task_create`'s, and Desk's own tools, tidy and migrations write cards through the file system, which the hook never sees. Copilot and Codex have no equivalent hook yet; the `task-lifecycle` skill says so and states the rule for them.

"Resume `<task>`" now continues the card's recorded next step. `using-superpowers-with-desk` says a next step that is one small, clear change needs no plan document, and that a plan that is genuinely needed goes in the task's own iteration folder in the desk, never in the operator's code repository or the desk root. The agent chooses subagent or inline execution itself instead of asking the operator, and the adapter states that Superpowers' `writing-plans` is not the host's Plan mode.

`desk_status` and the session-start boot script now share one health word (`runtime/health.js`). The boot script's `sync` result is recorded after each pull, so `desk_status` reports `degraded` with the same `sync: unresolved (...)` line and a fix when the last sync failed, instead of "ready" and "in sync" beside a boot that said `degraded`. A later good sync or a successful push clears it.

The startup hook's `Desk startup:` line now says the boot has not run and gives the exact command with its absolute path (`node <plugin>/mcp/scripts/session-boot.js`, with `--task` when the operator names one), and the hook's own checks are labelled `Desk boot pre-checks:`, so neither reads as a finished boot. The `session-start` skill shrinks to what the boot instructions do not already say, and shows how to derive the same absolute path when the line is not in context.

A task card's `repos` entry may carry a `url`. When a recorded local clone is missing, the boot instruction gives the exact `git clone <url> <path>` (or `gh repo clone owner/repo <path>`), and when there is no url it says exactly what to ask the operator and where to save the answer.

The boot-acceptance harness only flags a push to a remote other than the desk's own origin, lists the injected task in the fixture's track card, and uses a fixture desk and prompts that read like a real operator's, with no harness wording and no "Never enter Plan mode" rule.

Review fixes on the same change. The card guard now denies only a live card of the bound desk (`<desk>/<track>/<slug>/task.md`, matched by real path, case-insensitively, with `~` expanded, and by inode for a hard-link alias); a `task.md` outside a desk is untouched, an archived card keeps the status-only guard, and a card whose frontmatter no longer parses may be repaired by hand, which the deny message says. When the bound root cannot be determined the guard falls back to the nearest parent folder with the desk marker (`_meta/` plus `_archive/` or `desks/`). The clone instruction accepts only strict `https://`, `ssh://` and `git@host:path` urls, shell-quotes both arguments and runs `git clone -- <url> <path>`; anything else gets the ask-the-operator text. `note` and `next_step` skip fenced code blocks, end the next-step paragraph at a blank line, list item or heading, replace the first marker and drop later duplicates, keep CRLF, and date notes in the operator's local time zone. The recorded pull failure carries a timestamp and `desk_status` treats it as cleared once a later fetch or push succeeded, or after 24 hours; the status file is written by temp file and rename, and concurrent writers are last-writer-wins. The harness counts MultiEdit, and judges a bare `git push` by the folder it runs in.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.158 — 2026-10-01

The factory now records which harness ran each session with the same care as the model. Subagents record their agent type and the model that was requested, next to the model that actually answered, wherever the harness records them; the Claude Code root session records neither. Agent types that are not built into the harness, or that come from a private plugin, are published as `custom`, so a private agent name never leaves the machine.

Codex CLI sessions are now derived and captured. The deriver reads Codex rollout files, including the threads a session spawned, and the Codex activation registers `factory-end.cjs codex` on the Codex `SessionEnd` hook with a 3 second timeout. A Codex session is captured when it ends cleanly, and it is derived and published at the next Claude Code or Copilot CLI session start. Codex capture is unproven: no real Codex session has run on our hosts yet, so the format comes from the openai/codex source and from fixtures, and Codex runs the hook only once it trusts it. A Codex session that crashes never fires `SessionEnd` and is not captured. Codex session ids carry their creation time, so they are published as a per-machine keyed id.

Sessions whose local markers remain, about the last 30 days, are derived again once after this release (binding version 3), so they gain the new agent type and requested model fields.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.157 — 2026-10-01

Follow-ups to the code-repo done evidence check. A `pr` or `commit` URL must now have exactly the host's own shape: `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<N>` (a sub-page, query or fragment may follow), an Azure DevOps `.../_git/<repo>/pullrequest/<N>` URL, or, for any other host, a host that one of the recorded clones has a remote on. A repo name smuggled into another URL's path, or on an unrelated host, no longer matches. A commit that does not resolve, or is not pushed, now tells the agent to run `git fetch` in the recorded clone first, because a squash-merged PR's commit reaches the default branch only after a fetch. A card whose `repos:` lists plain names (`repos: [widgets]`) counts as naming code repos. A relative `local_path` is resolved against the desk root, not the process's working directory, by one shared helper (`resolveLocalPath`) that boot's repo checks use too.

`task_update` refuses to remove every repo from a card that names them, so one call can no longer empty `repos` and a second finish the task on `non_code` evidence. Two exits remain: set `status: cancelled` in the same call, or pass `repos_removed_reason` (one line) because the work turned out not to touch the repos. The reason is recorded on the card as `repos_removed: [{ name, reason, at }]`, and that call may not also set `status: done`; finishing is a separate call with `non_code` evidence that is not the card itself. Ports and logins are ignored when comparing an ssh remote's host with a PR URL's, and legacy `visualstudio.com/DefaultCollection/<project>` URLs are accepted.

`task-lifecycle`, `session-start` and `codex-onboarding` now say `desk_status` returns `sync` and the index and database fields only with `detail: true`, and the `using-superpowers-with-desk` routing table separates its two plan rows (subagent-driven or inline) so `executing-plans` is reachable.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.156 — 2026-10-01

Boot and status fixes from the sixth Haiku acceptance round. A task whose card lists `repos:` can no longer be finished on stand-in evidence: `task_update` and `task_archive` now accept only a `pr` URL in one of those repos (a fork route's upstream and fork both match; no network call) or a `commit` that resolves in a recorded local clone and is already contained in a remote-tracking branch, and refuse a desk commit, an unpushed commit, `ci_run` and `non_code`, each with an error naming exactly what to supply. A `non_code` proof may no longer be the task card itself. The `task-lifecycle` and `task-card-format` skills carry the rule, including that a task whose PR cannot be opened stays `blocked`.

The boot result's `instructions` are now the single list of next steps: the duplicate `actions` list and the `desk_export_line` field are gone, and the `export DESK=...` instruction (which cannot persist across an agent's separate shell calls) is replaced by one line giving the desk's absolute path to use directly. `desk_status` now answers compactly by default (`state` in boot's own words `ready`, `degraded`, `admitting` or `setup_required`; why and what to do when not ready; `search` as its own one-word index state that never makes `state` degraded) and returns the full payload only for `detail: true`. `using-superpowers-with-desk` now routes a task with no plan to `brainstorming` or `writing-plans`, never to `executing-plans`.

The readiness controller no longer hands its journal a state directory that sits under a symlinked ancestor (a home under `/home` linked elsewhere, `~/.cache` moved to another disk, macOS's `/var` to `/private/var`): it resolves the existing ancestors to their real path first, and still refuses a state directory that is itself a link or a file. Before this, such a machine lost the search index to "journal has unsafe state directory ancestry" and `desk_status` read degraded.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.155 — 2026-10-01

`scripts/session-boot.js` now restores the plugin's runtime dependencies the way the MCP server does, so the one-command boot works from an installed plugin folder. Before, the script ran with no `node_modules` beside it, could not load `gray-matter`, and so could not read the `repos:` lists in task cards: `push_accounts` came back empty and every card's repos showed "not validated". The script now restores the shipped runtime pack into the same cache folder the server uses (`DESK_RUNTIME_CACHE_DIR`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`), with the same atomic publication, before the boot code loads. A machine the pack does not cover keeps the dependency-free reader, and the "card repos: not validated" line now says why the restore failed.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.154 — 2026-10-01

Boot and status fixes from the fifth Haiku acceptance round. A desk sync that fails now says why: an unreachable origin, refused credentials, a diverged history, a timeout or a real conflict each get their own instruction, where every failure used to read as "resolve the git conflict" and sent agents to a `git status` that looked clean. An unreachable or refusing remote no longer moves stray files to `_cache/` either. The boot `instructions` now carry the push route for the named (or every active) task's repos whenever it is a fork, goes through a non-active account, or no account can deliver, naming repo, account and route; lines are grouped per repo (first few tasks named, then a count) and capped at five plus a summary.

`desk_status.readiness.state` is now one word (`ready`, `converging`, `degraded`, `unavailable` or `not_checked`) about the search index only, with the controller's own state name and convergence snapshot under `readiness.detail`; the raw `RECOVERING` beside an admission `ready` no longer reads as an outage. The task-status guard now denies any direct Edit, Write or MultiEdit that changes a task card's `status:` (not only `done`) and names the exact `task_update` call; body edits and new cards stay allowed. `superpowers-context.js` compares real paths when it decides it was started as a script, so it no longer exits silently when run through a symlinked path.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.153 — 2026-09-30

The startup hook's `Desk boot:` line no longer contradicts the one-call boot. The factory check used to add `Factory: this desk hasn't decided whether to contribute measurement data; ask the operator once (desk:session-start)`, which told the agent to raise consent before the operator's work and in noninteractive sessions too. The boot script's `instructions` now own that rule alone (consent comes after the operator's work, at most once, and never in a noninteractive session), so the check says nothing for an undecided store and `desk_doctor`'s Factory section words it the same way.
