A public store, or one whose visibility is unknown, gets the name and version of each plugin from a public source and a count of the rest in the new optional `refs.private.plugins`; an organization's private or internal store keeps every name. Markers, local facts and published files written before this change stay valid ([publishing transform](mcp/src/factory/publish.js), [published schema](mcp/src/factory/published-schema.js), [end hook](hooks/factory-end.cjs), [factory capture](docs/factory-local-capture.md)).

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.106 — 2026-09-28

Each session start now lists the open andon issues of every store the desk contributes to, in one line such as `Factory: 1 open andon issue in ourostack/factory (#41); ...`. The start-time delivery refreshes the list after each store's flush, keeping only issues the store's build opened for a plugin in the store's `factory.json` that no one labeled `andon-dismissed` ([andon-watch.js](mcp/src/factory/andon-watch.js)). The line therefore shows what the previous session's refresh found, and the `session-start` skill says to offer a `curator` pass, which handles andon first. The RFC now describes the kaizen loop and andon under the current rules ([agentic-engineering-v2-rfc.md](docs/agentic-engineering-v2-rfc.md), section 4): at least 6 independent session groups a side, computed from the confidence level; andon only for tracked plugins, on the latest version with enough jobs; the `andon-dismissed` label; and structured-only public cards filed after signoff. Section 9 records the first live check of card factory#39 and keeps draining the desks' friction under Still open. The andon fixture test now uses 6 single-session jobs a version, adds a case where too few jobs neither open nor close an alarm, and says that GitHub is simulated ([andon_regression.test.js](mcp/__tests__/factory/andon_regression.test.js)). The README describes `curator` as running on the operator's request.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.105 — 2026-09-28

Friction about the shared system now becomes a kaizen candidate, and the curator files candidates as kaizen cards after its signoff step. `friction_add` takes `about`: `setup` (the default) stays on the desk as before, and `system` records a candidate on the desk with a `title`, the `plugin` (default `desk`), a `friction_class`, and the `signal` and `evidence_jobs` when known; nothing leaves the machine. With `file_card: true`, which only the `curator` skill uses, the card is filed in the store the desk's facts go to, including the routing the session recorded from the overlays; when that route is unknown nothing is filed ([kaizen-file.js](mcp/src/factory/kaizen-file.js)). Free text never goes to a public store: a public card carries only the plugin (publicly distributed plugins only), the friction class, the measure and published job IDs, and this machine's plain local job IDs are refused. A private store's card also carries the title and body, still refused if they hold anything credential-shaped, a home or drive path, or an email address. A fingerprint keeps a retry from filing the same card twice, and at most five cards go to one store in a day. The `friction-management` skill says which friction is about the system, `lesson-capture` proposes candidates from the evaluator's waste without filing them, and `curator` becomes the kaizen worker: it handles open andon issues first, files candidates after signoff, ships each card's countermeasure, fills in `version` when the release lands, closes confirmed cards and reverts or re-plans not-confirmed ones.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.104 — 2026-09-28

Every Desk MCP tool now advertises a real JSON input schema ([tool schemas](mcp/src/tool-schemas.js)), with its properties, required fields and `additionalProperties: false`. Before, 17 of the 18 tools were listed as `{ properties: {}, additionalProperties: true }`, so Claude Code had no shape to build arguments from and sent `task_update`'s `frontmatter` as a JSON string, which the tool spread into the card as one key per character (`"0": "{"`, `"1": "\""`, ...). A test now fails if any tool is registered without declared properties, with a required field it does not declare, or with an object or list field that has no type. Object and list fields are typed: `repos` entries carry `name`, `local_path` and `mode`, and the work ledger's `operator_go`, `task_ref`, `carry_forward` and path lists have their shapes. Only the degraded server that runs when no compatible Node is found still lists tools with an open schema, on purpose, because every call there answers with the reason and the fix.

`task_create` now accepts and keeps `initiated_by` (`operator` or `agent`) and `origin_note`, which `desk:start-task` asks for; before, both were silently dropped. `desk:start-task` also says that `created`, `updated` and `schema_version` are set by the tool.

`task_update`, `track_update` and `desk_search` also check their object arguments themselves ([object input](mcp/src/util/object-input.js)): a plain object is used as is, a string that holds a JSON object is parsed, and anything else is refused with a clear error before the card is read or written, so a corrupted card can no longer be written. A bad `desk_search` filter says that nothing was searched and shows a filter example.

Session start no longer repeats a folder name that carries a secret's value.

- The `Desk startup:` and `Desk boot:` lines both hooks inject redact to `<redacted segment>` each path segment, branch name or word that looks like a password value, a 16+ character secret run, a known token prefix or an IPv4 address ([redaction](mcp/src/util/redact.js)). A path segment is judged whole, spaces included, so a folder named `set pw hunter2` is caught, and a sentence's closing full stop stays outside the marker. The machine's own home and temporary folders are exempt, and the boot line is withheld rather than shown unredacted if the redaction cannot load.
- `desk_status` now serves the active-task listing as `active_tasks` ([listing](mcp/src/desk/active-tasks.js)): non-terminal tasks by track, with track, task and desk names, titles and repository paths redacted the same way, a count of what it hid, and each task's repositories. `desk:session-start` and `desk:status` build their status blocks from it instead of globbing task cards, so hosts that gate shell commands do not prompt for it, and they never recover or quote a redacted name.
- The session-start desk sync is now `git pull --rebase --quiet`: without `--quiet` the pull printed a diffstat and a `create mode` line naming every folder another machine had created.
- Every task and track has a stable handle (`task-` or `track-` and ten hex characters, [handles](mcp/src/desk/handles.js)) in the listing, in `desk_doctor`'s organization findings and in the tidy report. `task_move` takes `handle` in place of `track` and `slug`, and `track_rename` takes `handle` in place of `track`, so a redacted folder gets an outcome name without anyone reading the old one; the tidy renames by handle instead of listing the folder's parent. A handle is a keyed hash of the folder's path; the key lives in Desk's state folder on this machine, not in the desk. `task_move` and `track_rename` also redact credential-like names in their results and errors.

This closes the live finding where a session-start status quoted the user and password in two task folder names. A topic word such as `token` or `password` alone is not redacted, per the name rule. Out of scope: `desk_status` still reports the desk root unredacted in `root`, because the agent needs the real path to work in the desk.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.103 — 2026-09-28

Tests: the protected-checkout inspection-budget test no longer depends on runner speed. Its slow Git now answers only long after the 2 s budget, so `git checkout topic`, `git pull -q origin other` and `git push -q origin HEAD:other` always take the budget path, and the test asserts the budget message for each. Before, Git answered in 1.5 s, so a fast runner decided the pull and push on content, and round 4 allows both.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.102 — 2026-09-27

Milestone 3a, task A3b (P0 correction to the protected-checkout guard, ourostack/desk#24): the guard now denies only a Git operation that moves a protected checkout's HEAD off its state branch, rewrites pushed history, or discards other sessions' work, and everything else passes. In a protected checkout, `add`, `rm`, `mv`, `commit`, amending an unpushed commit, every merge, `pull` and `rebase` onto the state branch's own upstream (with or without `--autostash`, `-c rebase.autoStash` or `-c merge.autoStash`), non-force pushes of any branch or tag, deleting another branch on the remote, unstaging or restoring named paths (`git restore [--source <commit>] <paths>`, `git checkout -- <paths>`, `git reset -- <paths>`, including globs such as `'*.txt'` and absolute paths inside the checkout), config writes to another file, `worktree add`, `worktree prune`, read-only Git, and plumbing that leaves HEAD and the tree alone (`update-ref` of other refs included) pass. The [policy](mcp/src/runtime/git-guard-policy.js) still denies leaving the state branch, `reset --hard`/`--merge`/`--keep` and HEAD-moving resets, unstaging or restoring any pathspec that covers the checkout root (no path, `.` or `..` spelled any way, `:/`, wildcard-only patterns such as `*` and `*/`, magic and exclude pathspecs such as `':!x'`, and the root as an absolute path such as `"$PWD"`, `"$(pwd)"` or `"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"`), plumbing that moves HEAD or discards work (`symbolic-ref HEAD <another ref>`, `update-ref` of `HEAD` or the current or state branch, `read-tree -u` with `--reset` or `-m`, `checkout-index -f` with `-a`, `--stdin` or root-covering paths), `clean`, `stash` (including `push`, `pop` and `drop`), forced, mirrored and pruning pushes and deleting the state branch on the remote, rebases onto anything but the upstream, force-moving or deleting the current or state branch, amending a pushed commit and forced removal of a protected worktree. Admission records the host's state branch beside the marker (`desk.stateBranch`), and it now marks a root only when it is a real desk (the desk layout, or a saved desk binding): three tests had bound `$DESK` to the checkout running the suite, which is how some code worktrees were marked protected.

Every denial names the concrete alternative, and credentials are redacted from it and from the hook's own error message: URL user information (`https://user:token@host`), token-shaped users in `url.<user>@host:` keys and scp-style addresses, `Authorization` values and tokens with a known prefix. Only a real HEAD move, rewind or discard names the worktree command, `git worktree add --detach "$HOME/<new directory>" <ref>`, which runs as written in Bash and PowerShell; ordinary desk writes are never sent to a worktree. When the target checkout is unknown or computed (`cd "$x" && git …`), operations that are safe in any checkout still pass. Code Desk cannot read (a script file, `source`, `curl … | bash`, `eval "$(…)"`) passes; inline code it can read (`bash -c "…"`, here-documents) is inspected like any other command. Text Desk cannot parse is denied only when it names a Git operation whose rule could deny it there.

The Bash inspector now reads brace expansion, process substitution, here-documents inside `$( )`, a newline after `&&`, `||` or `|`, `case` arms in groups, `set -e`, and directories the command itself creates. PowerShell Git inside groups, subexpressions, script blocks, control statements, functions and `& git` now runs as its own statement under the same rules, a group's value is its static answer or one unknown value, and code Desk cannot read passes unless its readable text names Git; a statement that names Git itself must still be a plain form, and that allowlist never fires in an unprotected checkout. One step budget (20,000 steps) and one 7 s time budget bound all inspection, and the hook answers at 9 s. Replayed against 22,880 recorded agent commands, the guard denies 6 in a protected desk (5 real HEAD moves, stashes or state-branch force-moves, and 1 whose computed directory no longer exists) and none in an ordinary repository; all 164 expected-allow rows from earlier reviews pass. The rule, threat model and command table are in the [hook header](hooks/protected-checkout.cjs) and [Protected checkouts](docs/protected-checkouts.md).

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.101 — 2026-09-27

Desk now asks each machine once whether to contribute to its desk's factory store. [`desk:session-start`](skills/session-start/SKILL.md) Step 2.7, and [`desk:first-run-bootstrap`](skills/first-run-bootstrap/SKILL.md) at its endpoint, ask when `desk_status` reports the resolved store as `undecided`, in plain words: what is published (durations, counts, tool kinds, plugin and model versions, public references), what never is (prompt, assistant or tool content, names, dates and times of day), that the store is public, and that the contributor's GitHub account is the visible author of the intake pull requests. The account is never assumed from gh's active account: the new `factory.js account --store <store>` asks GitHub about the store with each signed-in account's own token, prefers push permission, then an account that can fork, and never picks an Enterprise Managed User account for a store outside its enterprise; when no signed-in account can deliver, it names each account's reason and nothing is asked. `factory.js consent` now accepts an Enterprise Managed User login, which a work store needs, and a flush whose account cannot fork the store stops with the new code `account_cannot_deliver` instead of waiting on a fork that will never exist. Yes and no are both recorded with `factory.js consent`, and a recorded decision is never asked again; a noninteractive session neither asks nor records. The startup hook's boot line asks exactly when `desk_status` reports `undecided`, because both read the decision with the same code; `held`, `unreadable`, `yes` and `no` never ask.

`desk_status` and `desk_doctor` now report `factory`: the bound desk's store and how it was chosen, consent per store, undelivered and quarantined counts and the last flush result code, from a read-only [local status](mcp/src/factory/local-status.js) that carries no path, secret, account, intake ID, token, time or content; `desk_doctor` adds it as a `Factory` summary section. The MCP server reads the host's plugin set with the end hook's own scan, loaded from the installed plugin root because an installed server runs from a source mirror without `hooks/`. The [entrypoint](mcp/index.js) now names that root in `DESK_PLUGIN_ROOT` on every host, so Copilot and Codex, whose MCP configs pass no environment, route a desk to the same store as Claude and the hooks instead of reporting `plugin_scan_incomplete`; a test launches the server exactly as `.mcp.copilot.json` does. On the transition to `done`, `task_update` and `task_archive` write `factory_report: <link>`, the task's `factory.js job-link`, into the card when the resolved store has consent; the link resolves once the store merges, and done never waits for delivery. [`desk:task-lifecycle`](skills/task-lifecycle/SKILL.md) says so and [`desk:task-card-format`](skills/task-card-format/SKILL.md) documents the optional field. Cards without consent, `cancelled` cards and edits of a done card are written as before. See the [factory doc](docs/factory-local-capture.md#consent-status-and-the-report-link).

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.100 — 2026-09-27

A factory store's build now checks kaizen cards and pulls the andon cord ([factory-local-capture.md](docs/factory-local-capture.md)), and both claim a change only when the data can support it. `factory.js kaizen-check` reads each open issue labeled `kaizen`, parses its `yaml` card, and once the card names the version that first carries its countermeasure, compares the card's measure between finished jobs before and after that version. Jobs that share a session count as one independent group, and the 95% interval comes from 10,000 bootstrap resamples of those groups seeded with the card's number ([compare.js](mcp/src/factory/pipeline/compare.js)). A side needs at least 6 groups, the fewest for which a distribution-free 95% interval for a median can exist; the code derives that number from the confidence level. The card gets `confirmed` or `not-confirmed` only when the interval lies wholly on one side of zero, and its one comment otherwise says either "not enough independent jobs yet" or "no clear change so far". A card naming a job class the store cannot fill yet gets `job_class_not_published`, and a card whose calls fail is reported without stopping the others. `factory.js andon` watches only the plugins listed in the store's `factory.json`, which only a maintainer can change, and compares each one's latest version with enough jobs against the one before it on every quality measure and job class. It opens one `andon` issue per clear regression, titled with the job class, names any other plugins that changed with the same jobs, and closes the issue when the data or a later version no longer shows the regression. A reviewer can label an alarm `andon-dismissed`: the build then never reopens it but still posts its new numbers. The [store playbook](docs/factory-store-playbook.md) adds `factory.json`, a kaizen issue form, and a build whose issue steps run in their own job with only `issues: write`.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.99 — 2026-09-27

Every fresh Desk install now has a working browser. Desk declares a `desk-browser` MCP server on Claude and on Copilot, started by the new [`mcp/browser.cjs`](mcp/browser.cjs), so a server of your own named `playwright` stays yours. The launcher starts a copy of `@playwright/mcp` installed in Desk's per-user state folder, under the same compatible Node that Desk's bootstrap picks. The first launch installs it; later launches start it with no network call and refresh it from the `@latest` channel in a detached process, with no pinned version. Installs and refreshes share one lock and switch versions with an atomic rename, so sessions that start together never race, and every npm call runs with no retries and a short timeout, so an unreachable registry fails in seconds with a message naming it. The browser runs headless, so agents never take the operator's focus, and isolated, so concurrent sessions never share a profile. Playwright MCP writes its snapshots to the state folder, never into the session's project, and the launcher logs the version it started. It uses Chrome, or Edge when Chrome is not installed, and passes Chrome's path when it is only in `~/Applications`. `SETUP.md` now lists Chrome or Edge as a prerequisite and checks that the browser opens a page. Signed-in browsing still goes through the claims-based broker, and [`desk:cdp-headed-browser`](skills/cdp-headed-browser/SKILL.md) now routes signed-in work there even when the headless browser is healthy and explains the route on an install with no overlay provider.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.98 — 2026-09-27

The checkout guard now handles much longer shell loops before it gives up. Before a shell command runs, the guard walks its syntax to find Git calls that would move a protected checkout. A `for` loop whose body ran a command with an unknown exit status used to double the paths it walked on every iteration, so a loop over about ten values ran past the guard's 1,000-step budget and the guard blocked the whole command, which the host records as a failed tool call. The walk now merges identical states after each loop iteration (and after a `while` loop), so a loop costs steps in proportion to its length, and Git inspection stops once one Git call is denied ([shell-commands.js](mcp/src/runtime/shell-commands.js), [protected-checkout.js](mcp/src/runtime/protected-checkout.js)). The step budget itself is unchanged, so a loop still fails closed when its length times the steps per iteration passes 1,000: about 150 to 250 values, depending on the body. This is the countermeasure for kaizen card [ourostack/factory#39](https://github.com/ourostack/factory/issues/39), whose hypothesis is fewer tool failures per job.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.97 — 2026-09-27

Pull requests that change the offline evaluation suite pass the coverage gate again. The offline coverage hook resolved the vendored gauntlet TypeScript files as plain JavaScript modules so the coverage instrumenter would pick them up, which only works when those files are instrumented. When a change did not touch them, Node parsed raw TypeScript as JavaScript, and every offline test that imports one failed with `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '{'` (11 of 12 shards in run 36348056164). The [hook](../../evals/offline/__tests__/helpers/coverage-format.mjs) now loads an uninstrumented TypeScript file as TypeScript, so Node strips its types, and a producer test covers a change that leaves those files alone. The coverage shards also gained measured weights for the 75 offline test files ([coverage-shards.json](mcp/config/coverage-shards.json)): an offline-scope run is now bounded by its slowest file, `controller-publication.test.mjs` at about 392 s, with the other shards at about 129 s instead of piling onto it.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.96 — 2026-09-27

Accepted waste labels now reach the store, and finished tasks without labels are noticed at session start. The [flush](mcp/src/factory/flush.js) delivers local labels in the same intake pull request as facts: each becomes `labels/<job>/<session-id>.json` through `toPublishedLabels`, with the job keyed as its session's facts are, and passes the labels gate; a refused one is quarantined under its local key. A rejected intake PR quarantines its labels with its facts, and a stale one sends them again. Labels go only with their session's facts, already on the store's default branch or in the same batch, so a batch cap can never send labels the store would refuse for missing facts.

Labels whose session's facts are quarantined can never be delivered, so they no longer wait forever: the new outbox helper `holdLabels` quarantines them as `facts_quarantined`, with the quarantined facts file named in the record. The flush applies it before batching and after reading rejections, and the [evaluator](mcp/src/factory/evaluate-run.js) applies it to a session whose facts are quarantined, which then gets no brief and no longer keeps its job's evaluation request pending.

A new `labels` [boot check](hooks/boot-checks.cjs), second in the registry, runs when a store has consent. For retained evaluation requests it returns `Factory: N finished tasks have no waste labels yet; run the evaluator for them in the background` and starts a detached `factory.js evaluate --pending` through the compatible-Node launcher. For finished jobs with quarantined labels it returns `Factory: N finished tasks have quarantined waste labels that will not be delivered; tell the operator (desk:session-start)`, without a repair, and a request whose every session has quarantined labels is counted there rather than as waiting. [`desk:session-start`](skills/session-start/SKILL.md) Step 4.8 now owns what the agent does with these lines and with any `evaluate` answer, including starting one background `desk:observer` per ready job; [`desk:task-lifecycle`](skills/task-lifecycle/SKILL.md)'s done step points there.

Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`.

## 3.2.0-alpha.95 — 2026-09-27

Pull requests that change Desk no longer conflict with each other on the changelog or the version. Each one used to add its entry at the head of this changelog and bump fourteen version surfaces to the next free alpha, so every merge conflicted with every open pull request. A Desk change now adds a changelog fragment under [`changelog.d/`](changelog.d/README.md) and leaves the version alone. After the merge, the new [Desk release workflow](../../.github/workflows/desk-release.yml) runs [`release-desk.cjs`](../../scripts/release-desk.cjs) on `main`: it takes the next alpha on every release surface, folds the pending fragments into one entry here and commits the release to `main`, retrying on a newer `main` rather than overwriting it. Every merged change still ships as a new version. If a release fails, the workflow opens or comments on a "Desk release needs attention" issue and closes it after the next successful run, and a daily run releases whatever is still pending. [`check-release-integrity.cjs`](../../scripts/check-release-integrity.cjs) now requires from a pull request that changes Desk a new fragment the release will accept, checked by the same function the release uses (a top-level Markdown file, not empty, with no `#` or `##` heading), no other new file under `changelog.d/`, no change to a fragment another merge left pending, and an unchanged version, all judged from where the branch left `main`, so a branch behind a release is not blamed for it; the other plugins still bump their own version. The Superpowers refresh adds a Desk fragment instead of choosing the next free alpha from open pull requests, and dispatches the release after it merges, because a merge made with the workflow token starts no push-triggered run. This release itself still bumps the version by hand, because the rule in force is the one on the base branch. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.94 — 2026-09-27

The `desk MCP tests` workflow now finishes in two and a quarter to three and a half minutes instead of twelve to seventeen and a half, depending on whether the Windows job finds its cached dependencies. Its coverage gate ran the 237 instrumented test files one at a time in one job, and that one step took 11 to 17 minutes. CI now runs the files as twelve parallel shards, each on its own runner and still one file at a time under the same instrumentation ([runner](mcp/src/coverage/runner.js) `--shard <index>/<total> --output <dir>`). The `desk MCP test suite` job then admits the result with `--merge <dir>` only when every shard of the split is present once and passed, every shard measured the same changed files, and together they ran exactly the test files of one whole-suite run; the same 100% line, branch, function and statement gate then applies once to the combined raw coverage. Shards are balanced by each file's measured seconds in [coverage-shards.json](mcp/config/coverage-shards.json), which affects only the balance, and each shard records fresh timings for refreshing it. The Windows feedback job restores its installed test dependencies when the Node version and the lock are unchanged, because its `npm ci` became the longest step once the gate was sharded. `npm run test:coverage` with no arguments is unchanged. A failed shard still uploads its manifest and the merge job still runs, so the `desk MCP test suite` check fails and names the shard instead of being skipped, which a required check would count as passing. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.93 — 2026-09-27

Superpowers refresh: Desk now ships Superpowers 6.4.2, the selected payload of [obra/superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers/commit/8ca22dba9a94f28898bbce59f2537ff4d87c747d) (upstream version 6.4.2) copied byte for byte. Selected files: 17 changed, 4 added, 1 removed. [upstream-sources.lock.json](../../upstream-sources.lock.json) records every path and SHA-256. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.87 — 2026-09-27

Only the crew roster makes a desk a crew workspace. The one-time tidy used to treat any desk with `_meta/desks.md` as a crew desk, so a single-owner hub whose file is a cross-desk routing registry, or a spoke whose file only points at its hub, reported an unresolved person and was never tidied. The [crew roster reader](mcp/src/desk/crew-roster.js) now recognizes the roster by its table, the one whose header names both `alias` and `identity` (the table `desk:session-start` Step 2.6 documents), and parses it once; the [tidy](mcp/src/desk/tidy.js) uses it both to decide crew mode and to resolve the person, and asks no identity lookup of a desk without it. A `_meta/desks.md` the tidy cannot check or read (a dangling symlink, an unsearchable `_meta/`), or a `desks/` folder with no roster, still stops the tidy with the one-line crew message instead of tidying the crew root. The doctor's person-named-track check takes operator names only from that roster, and now also from every GitHub login in gh's local hosts config ([gh-logins](mcp/src/desk/gh-logins.js): `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, else gh's usual folder; read locally and bounded, tokens never kept), so a track named after the operator's GitHub login is refused and reported ([naming](mcp/src/desk/naming.js)), and [session-start](skills/session-start/SKILL.md) Step 2.6 says a hub registry or a spoke pointer is a single desk. A real crew desk keeps its behavior, including the one-line stop when no person resolves. Hub and spoke regressions cover the tidy, its migration, the doctor's names and the reader.

The workspace tidy resolves the bound desk root once to its real path and uses that one identity for task lookup, the report's name and `root`, the next boot's report lookup and the repair lock, so a desk bound through a symlink alias is no longer refused as an ownership mismatch and its repair report is no longer missed ([runtime](mcp/src/runtime/workspace-tidy.js), [boot checks](hooks/boot-checks.cjs)). A task card with no `repos:` field no longer stops the workspace tidy for the whole desk: it has no repositories besides the desk, and a card whose `repos:` cannot be read is counted in one issue and authorizes no removal, while every other card still counts. The session-start hook's root resolver ran nothing when its path went through a symlink, such as the macOS `$TMPDIR` or a symlinked `~/.claude`, so the hook said it could not resolve its root; [resolve-desk-root](mcp/scripts/resolve-desk-root.js) now compares real paths to decide it is the script being run.

Hooks no longer depend on the host's first `node` on PATH. The boot check's inspection combined its cancellation signals with `AbortSignal.any`, which Node 16 lacks, so on such a host every session start printed `workspace-tidy deferred; AbortSignal.any is not a function`; a local helper now combines them and removes its listeners. The boot path does no Node search: it starts the detached repair in the hook's own Node as a Node 16-safe launcher, which finds a Node that satisfies the MCP's engines range, chosen by the MCP bootstrap's own selection through [compatible-node.cjs](hooks/compatible-node.cjs), and re-executes the repair there. When none is installed, the launcher records it, written like the report (a private temporary file renamed into place, never through a symlink), and the next boot line says `last repair not started: it needs Node >=20.0.0 and none was found`. The boot-check registry's other detached children, the factory check's `finalize`, the desk-health fast-forward and factory delivery, start the same way through a `--compatible` launcher. The factory's end-of-session worker starts in a compatible Node too, searched for once per hook run, and is not started without one; its marker stays for a later retry. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.
## 3.2.0-alpha.86 — 2026-09-27

Factory delivery now exists. [`flush`](mcp/src/factory/flush.js) sends only transformed, gate-checked published facts to a store as one `Factory intake` pull request per machine per store: it resolves reference and desk visibility through a seven-day cache, keys a public or unknown desk's job IDs with the machine secret, quarantines refused files and store rejections (`factory-rejected: <code>` from the store's automation), marks files delivered once their exact blob is on the store's default branch, pushes directly with push permission or through a default-branch-only fork, and force-updates only the machine's `intake/<intake_id>` branch to the exact new commit. Every outcome is one of fifteen stable codes; the account token reaches `gh` only as `GH_TOKEN` and never an argument, status, error or file; a ten-minute-stale lock and a deadline checked at every `gh` call bound each attempt. [`finalize`](mcp/src/factory/flush.js) re-derives a finished job's sessions, lets the current session go quiet for five seconds, flushes and clears the request only once the job's files are delivered or quarantined. The [factory CLI](mcp/scripts/factory.js) adds `flush --store` and `finalize --job`, so the end-of-turn hook now starts finalize for pending requests.

Both session-start hooks now run an ordered [boot-check registry](hooks/boot-checks.cjs) within one 300 ms budget: `factory` asks the agent to ask the operator once when the bound desk's store has no consent decision and starts a detached finalize for finished jobs whose facts are not delivered; `desk-health` (A5) turns a degraded last start into one agent line and starts a detached fast-forward of a clean state branch that is behind its upstream, never touching a dirty or diverged branch; `workspace-tidy` joins as the third check. A check that overruns, including one that blocks synchronously past its budget, or fails is skipped for that start with its line and repair discarded, its real time counts against the total, and the skip is recorded in the protected factory status. Rejected intake PRs are read page by page, so every file of a 500-file batch is quarantined; the comment names no file, so good files in a rejected PR are quarantined too. A PR whose every code is `merge_conflict` or `unexpected_merge` quarantines nothing; its files go out again on a branch rebuilt from the store's current main, and the flush reports `intake_stale_retried` with the number of stale PRs it read. A mixed comment quarantines with its first data code. A Git call killed by its time limit or a signal never reads as a desk without a remote. The hooks' output is byte-identical when no check has a line and otherwise gains exactly one `Desk boot:` line. After their output is built they start [factory-start.cjs](hooks/factory-start.cjs) detached, which sweeps and flushes every consented store. Every GitHub interaction is tested against a fake runner; the live delivery proof is separate. The coverage gate now collects the instrumented suite's whole output: past spawnSync's 1 MiB default it killed the run after every test had passed. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.83 — 2026-09-27

A finished job's waste is now labeled by an independent evaluator. When a task reaches `done`, [`desk:task-lifecycle`](skills/task-lifecycle/SKILL.md) runs `factory.js evaluate` and, when a store has consent, starts a fresh `desk:observer` subagent in the background with only the brief paths; `done` does not wait for it. Each brief ([evaluator](mcp/src/factory/evaluate-run.js)) holds the session on the published clock, from the same code that publishes facts, plus the session log path and the plugin version and rubric the labels must carry. The observer labels stretches with the new [`desk:factory-evaluator`](skills/factory-evaluator/SKILL.md) rubric (value, support or muda, the eight wastes, mura and muri flags, evidence as exact fact intervals, no free text) and hands them in with `factory.js evaluate-accept`, which checks them against the brief with `{ code, path }` errors only and writes accepted labels to the local outbox at `labels/<store>/<job>/<session-id>.json`. `toPublishedLabels` maps them to `labels/<job>/<session-id>.json` with the same keyed job ID as the facts, and `pendingLabels` lists what the store has not received. Because the session that finishes a job is usually not derived yet at `done`, `evaluate` first records an evaluation request for the job in protected factory state; `factory.js evaluate --pending` prepares retained requests again, and a request is cleared only when every session of the job has ended and has accepted labels, or quarantined as `expired` or `not_opted_in`. Answers are checked against the session's own local facts, never the brief file, and facts that are gone are refused as `facts_missing`. Delivery of labels in the intake pull request and the boot check that runs `evaluate --pending` follow the transport slice. See the [factory doc](docs/factory-local-capture.md#waste-labels). Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.82 — 2026-09-27

The docs now agree with the [RFC](docs/agentic-engineering-v2-rfc.md). Section 9 is dated 27 September 2026 and says V2 is ready for evaluation, split into what works today, what is built but not yet live end to end, and what is still open, with each claim linked to its pull request. Section 4 now says what published factory facts carry: no identity and no time of day, only durations and offsets, random session IDs and, for a public desk, keyed-hash job IDs. The [work-measurement-ledger](skills/work-measurement-ledger/SKILL.md) skill says it is being retired in favor of the factory. Retired Work Suite names are gone from [repo-handling](skills/repo-handling/SKILL.md), [adopt-inflight-work](skills/adopt-inflight-work/SKILL.md), [task-card-format](skills/task-card-format/SKILL.md) and the [Crew README](../crew/README.md) (Crew 0.2.3), and the [dependency-activation stories](docs/dependency-activation-stories-and-criteria.md) are marked historical. The [root README](../../README.md) says vendored Superpowers follows upstream and the lock is evidence, not a pin, and [`SETUP.md`](../../SETUP.md) says Codex is not a supported target. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.80 — 2026-09-27

The factory build now writes rollups that show which waste costs the most across jobs. [`rollups.js`](mcp/src/factory/pipeline/rollups.js) adds `rollups/index.md`, `measures.json`, `muda.json`, `tool-kinds.json` and `coverage.json` to the `reports` output. They hold a Pareto of labeled muda time by waste type (overall, per job class and per plugin version), the measure catalog's median, 75th percentile and counted jobs (overall and per plugin version, host and job class), tool-kind calls and failures, and unattributed and label coverage. Jobs are grouped by plugin version, host, job class and waste type, never by contributor, and the output carries no date or time of day. Only finished jobs count: every measure of a job that is not done or cancelled, or whose lead time is censored, is excluded as `open_job`, and each group reports its open jobs. Only complete values count: unavailable and partial values are excluded and listed by reason per measure, never shown as zero. In the waste Pareto each session's labeled waste counts once, even when several jobs share the session, and each Pareto reports its shared sessions. Medians and p75 use the nearest-rank method. Output is byte-stable, with Pareto ties broken by waste name; the page lists plugin versions in version order. `build` now reads `labels/` as data: malformed labels or unexpected entries stop the build (`invalid_published_labels`, `invalid_labels_entry`, `labels_not_directory`), and labels that no longer match their session's facts are counted as unused with a reason. Published facts carry neither the task card's `kind` nor milestone 4's organization signal, so every job's class is `other` and `search_waste` is unavailable (`not_in_published_facts`). Per-job reports are unchanged. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.76 — 2026-09-27

Adds the [factory store playbook](docs/factory-store-playbook.md), the proven procedure for standing up any factory store in one admin sitting: repository and merge settings, labels, Actions settings including fork pull requests, the `main` ruleset with the required `factory-validate` check and a pull-request-only admin bypass, the `factory-validate`, `factory-merge` and `factory-build` workflow files verbatim, the store declarations a desk or overlay uses, the rule for merging by hand, and the three proof pull requests. The workflows decide who is a maintainer from the pull request author's permission on the store (`admin` or `write`), not from the author association, and a failed lookup counts as "not a maintainer".

[`validate-pr`](mcp/scripts/factory.js) now judges exactly what the merge lands. A `base...head` diff read only one merge base, so a crafted head with a second merge base could pass validation while its merge reverted files on `main` or deleted facts (review C1). It now builds the merge result with `git merge-tree --write-tree`, lists the base against that tree, reads new bytes from the merge result and previous bytes at the base, refuses a conflicting merge (`merge_conflict`) and a head carrying its own merge commits (`unexpected_merge`), and fails closed below Git 2.38 (`git_too_old`) or when merge-tree fails (`merge_tree_unavailable`). The reviewer's reproductions are [regression tests](mcp/__tests__/factory/validate_pr_merge.test.js). Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.74 — 2026-09-27

The Superpowers refresh driver no longer fails when `gh` prints nothing. On its first live run GitHub Actions was not allowed to open the pull request, and the fallback's `gh label list --search` printed no output at all (not `[]`) because the `upstream-refresh` label did not exist yet, so [`superpowers-upstream-pr.cjs`](../../scripts/superpowers-upstream-pr.cjs) stopped with a bare "Unexpected end of JSON input" before filing its issue. Every `gh` JSON read now treats an empty list as empty and reports any other empty or malformed output as a named error with `gh`'s stderr (tokens masked); label creation is idempotent. A scripted `gh` replays the live failure and now reaches the issue. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.68 — 2026-09-27

Desk now chooses its desk root without crossing from one desk to another. An explicit binding never falls back: when `--root`, the host/session root, the saved binding's `desk.root` or a set `$DESK` names a folder that is missing, is not a folder or cannot be read, Desk completes the handshake in `degraded:root_unavailable` instead of binding another desk. `desk_status` names the configured path, its source and, for a saved binding, the binding file; its fix says to restore the desk there, or to rebind with `desk:first-run-bootstrap` (or correct `DESK`). Reads and writes that need a desk refuse, and the same session upgrades to ready once the folder exists. Plain Desk no longer binds `~/ms-desk`: that home folder is consulted only while the ms-desk overlay is loaded next to Desk in the same Agency session, as source `overlay_home_fallback`, and such a session never falls back to the personal home folders, so a missing or non-desk `~/ms-desk` leaves it in setup mode. With no binding at all, `~/desk` and then `~/worker-workspace` still bind, but only when the folder has the desk layout (`_meta/` plus `_archive/` or `desks/`); an empty or unrelated folder leaves Desk in setup mode instead of binding it. `desk_status` reports their source as `home_fallback`, and setup mode still offers discovered desks during onboarding. The overlay's `~/ms-desk` follows the same layout rule. The startup hooks say the same, with the `DESK` remedy when `$DESK` is the source, and the boot check reports an unavailable bound desk instead of "no bound desk". `first-run-bootstrap` now also covers a rebind from `root_unavailable`, and its fresh-create keeps `_archive/` and `_meta/` in Git with `.gitkeep` files so a clone still has the desk shape. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.67 — 2026-09-27

Superpowers now follows upstream automatically. A weekly [refresh workflow](../../.github/workflows/superpowers-upstream.yml) (also run on demand) reads `obra/superpowers` at its default branch, copies the selected files byte for byte with the new `--update` mode of [`check-upstream-sources.cjs`](../../scripts/check-upstream-sources.cjs), and records every path and SHA-256 in `upstream-sources.lock.json`. A selected skill is vendored as its whole folder, so files upstream adds to it arrive and files it deletes leave; a new upstream skill is reported, not selected. When anything changed, [`refresh-superpowers.cjs`](../../scripts/refresh-superpowers.cjs) releases Superpowers at upstream's version (or the next patch) and Desk at the next unclaimed alpha, pushes `superpowers-upstream`, opens or updates its pull request, dispatches both CI workflows on it, and merges when every check passes; a failing check leaves the pull request open and files an `upstream-refresh` issue. Both CI workflows now accept `workflow_dispatch`, and a dispatched run checks version bumps against `origin/main`.

Setup no longer risks the operator's real Claude profile. [`SETUP.md`](../../SETUP.md) resolves the Claude config directory once (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, falling back to `~/.claude`) and every step uses it; the [Desk README](README.md) and [`session-start-migrations`](skills/session-start-migrations/SKILL.md) do the same, the [evaluation packet](docs/evaluation-packet.md) backs up and compares the evaluator's real config directory (recorded as `REAL_CLAUDE_DIR`) and asks for the release that carries this fix without naming a version, and a contract test rejects any setup step, skill or script that names `~/.claude` without the fallback. Tests that pinned the Superpowers version or commit now read them from the lock and manifests. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.65 — 2026-09-27

Factory stores accept waste labels as a second gated data path. An independent evaluator's labels for one job's session arrive as `labels/<job>/<session-id>.json` in the exact [labels v1 schema](mcp/src/factory/label-schema.js) (`desk.factory.labels/1`): the evaluator's Desk plugin version, model (through published facts' own model-ID validator) and rubric (1 to 999), non-overlapping stretches in start order on the session clock, each classed `value`, `support` or `muda` with a waste type for `muda` only, `mura` and `muri` flags and at least one evidence range, and `unavailable` codes from a closed set (`session_log_missing`, `facts_missing`). No field holds free text, and refusals use stable codes that never echo candidate content. Published facts and labels now refuse credential-shaped model IDs, plugin names and repository names as `credential_like`, using a factory copy of Desk's credential rules held in parity by a test. [`validate-pr`](mcp/src/factory/pipeline/validate-pr.js) reads the session's facts file from the tree the merge would leave, as data, and refuses labels whose facts are missing, ambiguous or invalid, whose job the facts do not bind, whose stretches run past the session, or whose evidence ranges do not equal an interval's start and end exactly. Labels that declare `facts_missing` need no facts file, and a replacement must come from an evaluator no older than the one it replaces. `build` ignores `labels/` until the waste rollups consume it. The [factory contract](docs/factory-local-capture.md) documents the path, the schema and the codes. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.59 — 2026-09-27

Flaky-test hardening and two load-related product fixes: tests that failed full coverage runs and CI only when the machine was loaded now wait for the event they check instead of a fixed sleep or a tight wall-clock race, use mocked timers where the behaviour is budget logic, and record their measured times in the test output. Covered: the detached end-hook derivation, the workspace-tidy boot check, the bootstrap's Node selection and Windows fixture cleanup, the truncated-index rebuild, controller re-election and restart, the stand-in Windows ACL provider, and the browser-context broker's lock, timeout and readiness waits. Product bounds stay asserted with stated headroom: the hosts' 3 s end-hook timeout, the 500 ms whole-check boot budget (exactly, under mocked time), and the broker's 5 s lock and 10 s request timeouts. Two product fixes came out of it. Every inspection Git call was killed at a fixed 2 s, so on a loaded machine the detached workspace repair retained owned worktrees with a bare "Command failed" reason, and the protected-checkout guard refused ordinary commands because it "could not inspect" them. [Git inspection](mcp/src/runtime/git-inspection.js) now takes a per-call limit and names a timeout; [workspace tidy](mcp/src/runtime/workspace-tidy.js) gives its Git calls 20 s; and the [guard](mcp/src/runtime/protected-checkout.js) shares a 7 s budget across one command's calls, so it always decides, failing closed with a named reason, before the hosts' 10 s hook timeout. Three in-process admission tests bound this repository's own folder as the desk, so every test run marked the real checkout protected and the installed guard then refused Git commands in it; they now bind temporary folders, and the [test preload](mcp/__tests__/_isolated_env.mjs) fails a run that changes the checkout's Git configuration. The [boot check](hooks/boot-checks.cjs) also accepts an `inspectionBudgetMs` option, with the 200 ms default unchanged, so detail tests can let inspection finish. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.55 — 2026-09-27

Adds `observer`, `worker`'s sibling agent named for Lean's process observer: it watches work it did not do, times it and classifies each step from evidence, and never does, fixes or certifies that work. Its identity-only bodies ([Claude](agents/observer.md), [Copilot](agents/observer.agent.md), [Codex](agents/observer.toml)) are registered beside `worker` in every host manifest and the [activation manifest](activation/desk.activation.json), never as the default. Its first skill, [`evaluate-release`](skills/evaluate-release/SKILL.md), works through a packet from a cold start on a named host, records each step with evidence and measured time, classifies every problem as a defect, a confusion or a gap, and reports without fixing anything. The [evaluation packet](docs/evaluation-packet.md) gives an outside evaluator setup from nothing on each host, seven scenarios with observable outcomes, and how to file findings as issues labeled `evaluation`; three scenarios are marked as landing by the evaluation. Everything under test is throwaway: the packet builds a new desk with a local remote, binds it through `DESK_ACTIVATION_CONFIG` and `DESK`, gives the sessions under test their own Desk state and cache (`XDG_STATE_HOME`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`), and stops the evaluation unless a preflight names that desk (on Claude Code, before the first session starts); every command block that writes fails closed inside the throwaway folder. `observer` runs from its own folder and profile and keeps its record in an evidence folder outside it; in a dry run a separate driver runs the packet and `observer` only observes. [Content contracts](../../scripts/test-desk-contracts.cjs) and the [host manifest verifier](../../scripts/test-desk-host-manifests.cjs) pin the bodies, the registrations, the skill and the packet. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.53 — 2026-09-27

Two [factory formula](mcp/src/factory/pipeline/formulas.js) edges are corrected. A job whose latest transition reopens it after `done` is open again: its lead time and flow efficiency are censored at the latest session end like any open job's, its status matches, and the earlier `done` stays in its transition history. When it closes again, lead time ends at the first `done` after the last reopen instead of the first close. A `tool_durations` gap, such as an open session's unfinished tool call, now marks the active-time family `partial` with the uncovered session count instead of `unavailable`; only sessions that lack their `turns` can make active time unavailable. Golden report outputs are unchanged. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.49 — 2026-09-27

The factory now includes a deterministic store-side pipeline. [`validate-pr`](mcp/src/factory/pipeline/validate-pr.js) accepts only added or monotonic updates to canonical published facts, keeps error output content-free, and lets the CLI inspect pull-request revisions through Git without checking out or executing candidate files. [`build`](mcp/src/factory/pipeline/build.js) normalizes validated facts, places sessions on each hashed job clock, computes classed lead, queue, active, busy, parallelism, within-job concurrency, waits, flow-efficiency and rework values, and emits byte-stable JSON plus the exact four-question Markdown report.

The [factory CLI](mcp/scripts/factory.js) adds `validate-pr`, `build` and `job-link`. The generated index reports job measures and coverage across bound and unattributed sessions, hosts, unavailable field/reason rates and plugin versions. Synthetic fixtures cover both hosts, open and closed jobs, shared and unattributed sessions, missing offsets, public/private references, repeat-build identity, hand-computed formulas and generated-output privacy scans. Missing wait, retry and duration data is published as `unavailable` with its reason, or as `partial` with the uncovered session count, never as a measured zero. Flow efficiency and lead-time shares use activity clipped to the lead-time window, with work before the task card reported separately. `build` publishes an empty index for a store without facts and ignores dotfiles under `facts/`, and `validate-pr` treats any non-fact path, including one under `facts/`, and any maintainer removal of a facts file as maintenance that is never merged automatically. Store workflows and transport remain owned by later milestone tasks. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.48 — 2026-09-26

M3-7 affected re-review correction: [marker reads](mcp/src/factory/marker.js) now compare the opened descriptor with both the post-read descriptor and current pathname. [Enumeration](mcp/src/factory/outbox.js) retains the protected directory's identity across repair, reading, return and pruning, and defers leaf protection until the read is bound to that directory and leaf. A swap to an external directory followed by restoration cannot supply a marker or trigger repair/pruning of that external record.

[Sweep and file-based derivation](mcp/src/factory/derive-run.js) use the protected marker reread under the session lock, never a newer-looking enumerated copy or a vanished file's stale contents. [Deterministic regressions](mcp/__tests__/factory/marker_identity.test.js) cover swap-out/open/swap-back, corrupt/expired external records, protection-time directory replacement and stale copies at use. Existing Windows ACL repair remains in place; no hook or test budget changed. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.47 — 2026-09-26

M3-7 review corrections: [marker enumeration](mcp/src/factory/outbox.js) now shares direct-read protection, byte limits and filename/session identity checks, including protection repair on native Windows. Capture, derivation and completion requests reject state beneath the bound desk even without Git and through symlink aliases. The [end hook](hooks/factory-end.cjs) treats every Claude registry cap as incomplete, refuses unsafe activation metadata through the canonical resolver and supervises synchronous work in a separate process under the unchanged deadline.

[Derivation](mcp/src/factory/derive-run.js) rechecks quietness and source identity under its lock, refuses changed snapshots and honors newer protected markers. [Review regressions](mcp/__tests__/factory/review_fixes.test.js) reproduce the five findings, including an actual FIFO, a blocked worker, protected-state refusal and concurrent resume/late-shutdown writes. No hook budget was increased. Ships `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6`; native dependency payload unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.46 — 2026-09-26

Session capture now connects the host lifecycle to the [protected local factory outbox](mcp/src/factory/outbox.js). The [end hook](hooks/factory-end.cjs) records bounded, content-free metadata on Claude `Stop`/`SessionEnd` and Copilot `agentStop`/`sessionEnd`, then starts detached derivation only on session end. It imports the [canonical desk resolver](mcp/scripts/resolve-desk-root.js) without printing startup output and retains routing and launcher context before host plugin folders disappear. ## 3.2.0-alpha.45 — 2026-09-26

A4 fix round 2: [workspace-tidy evidence](mcp/src/runtime/workspace-evidence.js) now stores each unacknowledged resource once and reconstructs its derived views on read. The reader and writer share the same 1,048,576-byte limit; acknowledged history stays in canonical accounting rather than accumulating in the local report. [Repair admission](hooks/boot-checks.cjs) checks pending and both final branch dispositions before cleanup, and every write checks capacity before replacing the file. Full capacity refuses cleanup while leaving evidence readable and acknowledgement able to make room.

The previous ownership, hidden-edit, endpoint, traversal and process-budget fixes remain in place; the bound-desk symlink-alias limitation remains deferred and fail-closed. ## 3.2.0-alpha.44 — 2026-09-26

A4 review correction: [workspace cleanup](mcp/src/runtime/workspace-tidy.js) refuses `assume-unchanged` and `skip-worktree` index states that can hide uncommitted bytes. [Exact-resource claims](mcp/src/runtime/workspace-claim.js) coordinate version-2 receipt creation, revocation and reacquisition with cleanup through absence readback; ancestry-merged branch deletion compares the released HEAD atomically. Task A4: [close-out ownership](skills/task-lifecycle/SKILL.md#close-out-at-every-ownership-boundary) now applies when every task, iteration or delegated assignment ends. Task A3, second review correction: the [Bash inspector](mcp/src/runtime/shell-commands.js) recognizes multiline and empty `case` forms after whitespace, gives an unmatched or empty case its own successful status, and treats every leading assignment word as scalar before splitting command arguments. The [PowerShell inspector](mcp/src/runtime/powershell-commands.js) inspects executable interpolation before treating a quoted result as data and retains both reachable directory, variable, environment and status states when an unknown external result controls `&&` or `||`.

Unknown or ambiguous option spellings cannot clear an already recognized mutation. ## 3.2.0-alpha.41 — 2026-09-26

Task A3 review corrections: [Git inspection](mcp/src/runtime/git-inspection.js) now uses a trusted absolute executable from standard host installation directories, never candidate `PATH`, and separates the modeled environment from the inspection-process environment. Only modeled Git location variables cross that boundary; candidate executable-search, loader and configuration overrides do not. The [review regressions](mcp/__tests__/runtime/protected_checkout_review.test.js) use a harmless executable sentinel, including a proposed command that explicitly names absolute Git, and require that inspection never runs it.

[Operation-specific option parsing](mcp/src/runtime/git-guard-options.js) accepts unambiguous Git option abbreviations and consumes option values, so `restore --sour` and `branch --forc` are guarded while `branch --format -f` remains read-only. Forced worktree removal reads the victim's policy without the issuer's `GIT_DIR`.

The [Bash inspector](mcp/src/runtime/shell-commands.js) handles ANSI-C quoting, unquoted variable field splitting, `case` arms and unreachable commands after `exit`. A [separate PowerShell interpreter](mcp/src/runtime/powershell-commands.js) handles case-insensitive variables and location parameters, assignment, `sl`, subexpressions and the correct nested-shell grammar. Claude registers both `Bash` and native `PowerShell`; Copilot's PowerShell hook command uses a cross-platform path. Unknown computed target locations produce explicit inspection errors instead of being treated as unprotected. [Documentation](docs/protected-checkouts.md) states the trust and grammar boundaries. Ships the unchanged `desk-mcp@1.4.0-alpha.6` dependency payload; native packs and snapshot source scope are unchanged.

## 3.2.0-alpha.40 — 2026-09-26

Milestone 3a, task A3: [protected-checkout shell hooks](docs/protected-checkouts.md) for Claude `PreToolUse` and Copilot `preToolUse`, registered at plugin scope with no parent/subagent exemption. The [shared guard](mcp/src/runtime/protected-checkout.js) denies only the listed Git operations on a locally marked target and returns the exact worktree guidance. The [shell inspector](mcp/src/runtime/shell-commands.js) resolves quoted paths, chained directories, Git directory options, wrappers, aliases, substitutions and control flow without executing the proposed command; the documentation states the boundary for arbitrary computed code. Human Git commands bypass no new Git hook because none is installed.
